- "Two in one day. That has to be a record." Huoyan is enjoying himself.
- "Kage was unaware Huoyan had any personality outside of…dictator."
- The misunderstanding: Huoyan wrote the class on the board "because the staff said that I was not advertising my class enough." Even Huoyan is under pressure to market himself. Then: "It turns out you were right! The class is mandatory for you now." That's a great joke.
- "Who would want to see Huoyan twice in one day?" said out loud, in front of him. "Ahem."
- "Both Kage and Cheyenne looked very uncomfortable seeing Huoyan smile."
- Huoyan's card: **Phoenix.** "A walking flame made flesh!" Flaming feathers he can fire or block with. Weakness: "If water gets on his wings, the flames die out, and it takes a while for that spark to truly ignite again!"
- **The telekinesis rule.** "This is…just basic telekinesis. Most people with enhancements that aren't mutations, body alterations like my wings, are capable of doing this. I'm one of the lucky ones. I have a body alteration and control of an enhancement as well. It's like I have two in one." That's the book's most important worldbuilding sentence about powers. It creates two categories: body alterations (no telekinesis) and controlled enhancements (telekinesis over what you produce). It also creates a third category: people who have both.
- "I had multiple lessons on it last ye-...ah. Right. You learned nothing last year. How could I forget so easily?" A great teacher burn.
- The lesson plan: focus on a single object; "do not attempt to change the shape and form of the objects you create yet. That will be the next lesson."
- Cheyenne: "It's very possible your power can't do telekinesis, y'know? I'm pretty sure mine can't… Easy, you don't wanna grab a bull's horns! **Calm down, partner.**" "Partner" becomes her name for everyone, and eventually Kage's name for her.
- Kage: "What, you think I don't have what it takes?! Well, listen here, 'partner.' I'm not like you! I-" He catches himself. "I didn't ask." Half growth: he stops the insult but still shuts her down.
- "Kage looked up at Huoyan, and all he could see was judgement." Projection again, like "the dude who messed up being a hero" in 1.2.
- The attempt: "**His hair suddenly stood up on end.** His eyes widened and for a millisecond, the glue ball floated. Then, Kage coughed. His eyes shut, his hair fell back into place, and his legs shook." Hair standing on end becomes Kage's telekinesis "tell." Hair standing on end is also what static electricity does.
- Huoyan, "his perfect icy exterior" breaking (an "icy" fire-man): "He should've just failed! Simple telekinesis like that shouldn't cause even the most rookie student to collapse. **Something's off.**"
- "Kage was unresponsive, but his body continued to shake and vibrate." Vibrating, like current running through him.

**What it's doing.** It establishes the telekinesis rules, introduces Cheyenne, cracks Huoyan's ice, and plants the biggest mystery about Kage's body.

**What works.** Cheyenne's intro, Huoyan's comic timing, and the telekinesis worldbuilding are all strong. The collapse is a great chapter ending: it's scary, and it's a question.

**What I'd push on.** The telekinesis rule is explained once, here, quickly, and then the book relies on it heavily (Ailani's rotating sigil, Rebento's rainbow eyes, Kage's floating pillars). It would help to repeat the core rule once more later, maybe when Rebento's tell is explained in 4.5, so readers remember why telekinesis is special. Also, the convulsing-and-vibrating detail is so strange that readers will expect an explanation. It's been 3,000 lines. Even a hint would help. (See Theories: "Kage's power is more than adhesive.")

**Score: 7.5/10.**

## 1.11 Bow To Your King

*1,201 words. The nurse's office, the same afternoon.*

**What happens.** Kage wakes in the nurse's office with an IV in his arm and blue hair wrapped around it: Zulfiya Shifo, a classmate whose hair heals. Huoyan, frantic, says Kage has been training nonstop for four months and needs rest. Kage rips out the IV and delivers the book's most important monologue: everyone thinks he's a failure, somebody "paid for my invisibility with their life," and "people will call me King Kage one day. That's not arrogance, by the way. It's a spoiler." Zulfiya, moved, starts calling him "your highness" and stops Huoyan from forcing more healing on him. Kage leaves. Zulfiya teases Huoyan about having a new favorite. Huoyan: "I haven't told Anubis everything."

**Details I caught.**

- Zulfiya's card: **Mending Mane.** "Full control of her hair," which heals by wrapping people slowly. Every strand moves "like fingers." The hair is normal-strength and "torn or burned hair takes a while to grow back." Her hair can also *read* bodies, as Abby says in 2.1 ("a girl who can see stuff when she wraps her hair around you").
- "I am not done healing you yet. Please refrain from more motion." Formal and calm.
- Cheyenne is "fixing her hat, refusing to make eye contact with him." He hurt her feelings in 1.10.
- Huoyan: "you've apparently been training nonstop for **the past four months**!" (Two plus two months: the calendar checks out.) "That is the potential I've seen in you the whole time I've taught you." He's always seen it.
- "When was the last time you took a day to yourself?"
- "Four hours. No, four hours and twenty minutes." Zulfiya corrects herself to the minute. Orion cites this in 3.4 as proof of how precisely she can read bodies.
- Kage rips out the IV.
- Huoyan: "This level of obsession is not okay! Where is this coming from?!"
- **The King Kage monologue.** Every sentence works. A few I want to point to:
  - "Last year, I stayed invisible on purpose. Didn't unpack, didn't try, didn't give anyone a reason to expect anything from me." "Didn't unpack" echoes the Prologue's bag.
  - "But then this year started, and that night happened." That confirms the store was at the start of Year 2.
  - "**Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life.**" One of the best lines in the book. He doesn't say "I failed to save someone." He says his laziness had a price, and someone else paid it.
  - "'He can't be a hero. Move him to general studies before he gets someone else killed.'" Three chapters later he finds almost exactly that sentence written on a photo of himself in Anubis's files. His paranoia was accurate.
  - "'Why is Anubis wasting his time on that failure?'"
  - "Just the kid who froze." In the Prologue, he didn't freeze. He grabbed a wrist and the awning fell. The public story and the real story are different, and he's adopted the public one.
  - "**People will call me King Kage one day. That's not arrogance, by the way. It's a spoiler.**" The book's most quotable line. Cheyenne recites it word for word in 3.9.
  - "I need that soul up above looking down and knowing, really knowing, that I became a real hero." The victim has no name. Kage calls them "that soul up above."
  - "So please. Do not tell me what I should be doing." He says nearly the same thing to Lance in 3.2: "DO NOT TELL ME TO CALM DOWN!"
- "Huoyan went to answer with a lecture, but for once, nothing was coming out."
- Cheyenne "lifted her hat as she stared at Kage's face. His eyes looked like they were glistening with tears he wouldn't let fall. She gave a small nod to herself." She decides to believe him. She tells him so in 3.9.
- Zulfiya "usually hated people with egos, but… it sounded like he believed everything he said." "**As you wish, your highness.**" The royalty motif starts here.
- Zulfiya to Huoyan: "**If he doesn't wish to be healed, he can't be forced.** Let the king be." A healer stating the principle of consent to treatment. It becomes the moral backbone of Frostbite's accusation in 2.10: Thanatos healed Kage "without consent."
- "Sorry to disappoint, your majesty," with a bow. The chapter title.
- "Ugh. You guys are boring. I'm going for a run." He slams the door.
- Zulfiya calls Huoyan "**Xiansheng** Huoyan." That's Mandarin for "Mr." or "teacher." She's the only student who addresses him in his own language.
- Cheyenne: "It's kind of freaking me out seeing you act like a person."
- Zulfiya: "Is he your favorite?! Has he overthrown your favoritism towards Lucifer?! Are you and Anubis fighting over which agency he'll join?!" So both men have agencies, and they compete for students.
- Huoyan: "Teachers do not have favorites. I loathe all of my students equally, and **I haven't told Anubis everything.**" What hasn't he told him? It's never answered.
- Zulfiya: "he's certainly my new favorite classmate."

**What it's doing.** It's Kage's thesis statement: who he is, what he lost, what he wants, and why he can't stop. It also starts the royalty motif and the consent thread.

**What works.** The monologue is the best speech in Section 1. It's specific, it's angry, and it reveals a kid who has accurately predicted what everyone thinks of him. Zulfiya's "your highness" turns a self-aggrandizing line into something affectionate. Huoyan's slip at the end is a great hook.

**What I'd push on.** The monologue is long, about 400 words. It's earned, but you could cut the middle third (the "Nope! You know what they said instead?" section) without losing anything, because "Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life" and "It's a spoiler" are doing all the work. And "I haven't told Anubis everything" is now one of the oldest unanswered lines in the book. Pay it off before the tournament ends, or at least have Huoyan almost say it again.

**Score: 9/10.**

## 1.12 Whatever It Takes

*1,759 words. An alley in the city, that night.*

**What happens.** Exhausted but refusing more healing, Kage runs until his legs give out and screams at them. A man in a suit steps out of an alley: the villain Shifting Horror, whose power lets him absorb and reshape matter. Kage doesn't recognize him. The man, who calls himself Thanatos Augustus, reads Kage's exhaustion perfectly, tells him "animals that never stop running don't evolve. They die," and offers to train him in secret in exchange for "a few favors." Kage hesitates, thinks about heroes as branded weapons, and shakes his hand.

**Details I caught.**

- "His pride just wouldn't let him" accept more of Zulfiya's healing.
- "Fuck! C'mon, c'mon! I know you guys can keep this up! We got this; don't just die on me right now!" He's yelling at his own legs.
- "Hello there." The villain's first words. Possibly an Obi-Wan nod, possibly just polite. Either way, it's calm in a way nothing else in the chapter is.
- **The villain card changes register.** "This man is the villain Shifting Horror. His enhancement is Bio-Forge." No exclamation points. No "!" anywhere. Flat, declarative sentences. Every hero card in the book is a cheerful broadcast; every villain card is a police report. It's one of the smartest formal choices in the book.
- Bio-Forge: cellular control of "density, shape, new appendages"; his cells "break down and absorb whatever they touch, biological or inorganic"; regeneration. Costs: overdrive "and his body begins failing from within"; some inorganic materials "reject integration entirely"; "losing [focus] brings migraines, then blackouts." The card ends: "**He is never finished consuming.**" Hunger is his whole nature, which is why the rebuild in 2.7 leaves him "starving."
- He absorbs a brick wall into his finger and taps Kage's shoulder with it.
- "There was a twitch in his hand he couldn't quite place." Five paragraphs later: "the same hand that had twitched earlier." His hand knows something he doesn't.
- Thanatos's look: six feet tall, "a large striking scar on his face that trailed down to his neck," purple eyes, black hair "that faded into white on the ends," a black suit and shirt with a white tie. He "rubbed the scar with his thumb." A habit. The scar has a story.
- "There is no need to destroy yourself for a few inches of progress."
- Kage: "I think they call it enhancement. I just call it a power usually." He's been in the program for a year and a half. It's a joke about his laziness, but it's a stretch.
- "I don't have some power that just cranks my muscle mass up thirty percent." Whose power is he thinking of? Reina's power does exactly that (Heat Charge), but he doesn't know her power yet. It reads as random envy.
- "I'm sticky, so I have to make up the difference everywhere else."
- The narration's best paragraph in Section 1: "He saw the micro-tension in his jaw, the tremor under determination, **the way ambition propped up exhaustion like splints on a failing structure. A boy trying to outrun collapse by sprinting toward it.**"
- "You speak like someone who's racing toward the top of a tower with no safety line, even as the steps crack under your feet."
- "You're chasing strength the way a starving animal chases meat, and animals that never stop running don't evolve." Then, alone on its own line: "They die." "Not yelled. Not dramatic." The paragraphing makes it land.
- "My name is **Thanatos Augustus**." New in Draft 3 (he used to be "Shioya"). Thanatos is the Greek god of death. Augustus means "majestic" and was the first Roman emperor's title. Death the Emperor, recruiting the boy who wants to be King.
- "And I didn't stop you, you stopped yourself." Manipulators make you feel your choices were your own.
- He absorbs "a sliver of rust and steel," then stands "close enough that leaving meant going through him."
- "**So the real question is, do you want to run past me or run with me?**"
- "He always enjoyed watching how ambition bends before it breaks."
- Kage: "I enjoy the chaos of combat and many teachers seem to try and water that part of me down."
- Thanatos: "Kid. I run in chaos. I'm probably one of the few people on this earth who actually understands what you're feeling. You're not just fighting to grow, you're fighting a system that works against everything you naturally are. The way your mind moves, the way you operate, that's not a flaw to correct. That's something to sharpen." This is textbook grooming: I understand you, they don't, the system is against you, your flaws are gifts, let's do this in secret.
- "I will train you when no one is looking."
- "I know you don't want people to view you as '**potential man**' forever." He uses the teachers' prayer word as an insult.
- "All you have to do is a few favors for me… All you have to do is shake mine."
- Kage's voice goes "slow, very different from how he normally spoke." The book shows his hesitation through rhythm.
- "It's nothing that conflicts with what you already want. That is all you need to be made aware of for now."
- **The justification:** "He thought of the heroes he knew in this world, **weapons who smiled because it was good for the branding.** His mind raced as he thought of the possibilities of the heroes at the program: Huoyan and Anubis. Were they cogs in the machine as well? Was he being built to be a logo?" The Prologue's "smiling weapons with brand logos" returns at the exact moment Kage uses it to justify the worst choice of his life. That's beautiful structure.
- "In his mind, this was the hand that promised true growth." Hands again. The hand that held air now holds a villain's.
- "Kage's heart wasn't on fire, but it beat like he'd turned a page for himself for the first time in a while."

**What it's doing.** It's the book's turning point. Kage chooses a villain mentor because no hero has offered him what this man offers: to be seen, and to be told his chaos is valuable.

**What works.** Thanatos is a fully formed villain from his first line. His speech is persuasive because it's partly true, and the book knows that. The flat villain card is a brilliant formal choice. And the choice is motivated: Kage has been told "potential" for a year, and here is someone who sounds like he means it.

**What I'd push on.** Kage asks Thanatos's name and says "I'm Kage Ozul" with almost no suspicion of a scarred stranger who absorbs walls. That's in character (he's exhausted and flattered), but one more beat of real fear (the hand twitching again, or him noticing the brick finger) would make his choice feel more like a choice and less like drift. Also: the "thirty percent muscle" line is so specific that readers will try to decode it. If it doesn't refer to anyone, make it vaguer.

**Score: 9/10.**

## 1.13 Partner, Not Pawn

*1,566 words. The same alley, the next night.*

**What happens.** Kage returns on time to the minute. Thanatos introduces him to Frostbite, an ice villain in a glowing suit. Kage recognizes him but can't place where, and his hand "curled around nothing." Frostbite calls him a pawn. Kage insists he's a partner, gets pinned to a wall with ice and punched, then glues Frostbite's mask. Frostbite tries to kill him; Thanatos takes the ice spike through his own shoulder, absorbs it, and orders Frostbite to back off. Kage's first task: photograph whatever he can find on Anubis. After Kage leaves, Thanatos admits Kage is a pawn who must believe he's a partner. Frostbite threatens to kill both of them if the boy tries him again, "I don't care what the guy in the chair may do to me."

**Details I caught.**

- "He showed up at the same alleyway at the exact same time as yesterday, down to the minute." Punctual for villains. Late for nothing at school.
- Thanatos "patted his head once or twice." The first of many paternal head pats from the wrong people.
- "Kage felt like there were other presences around, but when he scanned the area, he couldn't find anyone else." That's Nerezza, hidden in the shadows. She tells him in 2.6 that she saw him in this alley.
- Frostbite: "a glowing blue and black suit." "This one he recognized, but he couldn't quite put his finger on where. **His hand curled around nothing.**" This happens three times in the book, and every time, it's about Frostbite. (See Theories.)
- Frostbite's card: **Polar Shift.** "Total creation and control over ice." Cost: "Frostbite's enhancement gives him, well, frostbite. Frostbite forms on his body from the inside out." Weakness: "Fire and explosions are the best way to take him down." Two of the students in this book are fire users, and one makes explosions.
- The villain card has one joke ("well, frostbite") but keeps the flat register.
- "Horror…who is this?" Frostbite calls Thanatos "Horror." Thanatos calls him "Frost."
- "I'm Kage Ozul. Pleasure to meet you, sir." Polite to villains, rude to teachers.
- "Horror, you recruited a random child?" "Of course not, Frostbite. He's not random; I just met him yesterday." Great comic logic.
- Kage's doubt: "Was it all worth it just for strength? His thoughts began to linger, only to come to a halt at a single word said by Frostbite: pawn." His ego saves him from his conscience.
- "I am not a pawn. Thanatos here told me that we'd be partners… I will not sit here and be under anybody's thumb."
- The ice pillar, the pinned limbs, the punch, the nosebleed: "The back of Kage's head hit the bricks behind him."
- Kage, smiling through blood: "Sorry to ruin your '**smoldering aura farm**,' but I'm nobody's pawn." "Aura farming" is current slang for posing to look cool. It dates the book to right now, in a good way.
- "His arms were pinned, but his fingers weren't." Glue to the face.
- Frostbite: "**You sticky fucking freak**…I'll kill you!" That's Lucifer's exact insult from 1.7. Two characters, same three words. Either it's a coincidence, or it's a clue.
- Thanatos takes the spike: "The biomass within him took it in… his shoulder completely turned into crystallized ice." "**Sorry to give you the cold shoulder, Frost.**"
- Thanatos "spoke like a father talking to his child": "I will not be protecting you again. You are to stand on your own two feet… **I am not your teacher, or your savior. And I'm certainly no hero.**" He says he won't save Kage again. He saves him twice more.
- The task: "Khaled Nasser. Anubis." Kage "flushed a bit," then "deadpan": "Alright, I'll get it done." He's spying on his idol.
- "Besides his obvious abrasiveness, there was something about Frostbite he really didn't like. He couldn't name it."
- The reveal: "He is a pawn to us, but he can't know that. He needs to think we view him as an equal so he doesn't want to revolt. We don't need all of the hero program coming after us more than they already are."
- Frostbite: "the boss could always use extra info, but really, I just want to give him tasks that make him uncomfortable. Let's see how loyal he is."
- "If that boy tries me again, I will murder both of you. **I don't care what he…**" He stops. "I don't care what **the guy in the chair** may do to me." Two things here. First, "what he" implies the boss has an interest in Kage specifically. Second, "the guy in the chair" is our only description of the boss. A wheelchair? A throne? A desk chair? It's a great, strange image.

**What it's doing.** It introduces the villain organization, makes Kage's choice dangerous, and reveals to the reader (not to Kage) that he's being used.

**What works.** Kage standing up to Frostbite while bleeding is brave and stupid in a way that's completely him. Thanatos taking the spike is a great power demonstration and a character beat at once. The private conversation is classic dramatic irony, and it's earned.

**What I'd push on.** Frostbite is established here as volatile and cruel, and in 2.10 he becomes apologetic and almost fatherly. That shift is the point (and it's set up by "the other half" of his reasons), but it would help to plant one small softness in this chapter. A pause before the punch, or a glance at Kage's face when he says "kid." Right now, readers might feel 2.10 came from nowhere.

**Score: 8/10.**

## 1.14 Anubis-pheric Pressure

*1,366 words. Anubis's office, the next morning.*

**What happens.** Kage casts a key from glue and breaks into Anubis's office. It's bare: white walls, empty shelves, one Anubis action figure. The computer is locked, but the desk has folders: "Black Market," "Villain Encounters," "Student Prospects." Kage photographs everything, including notes on his classmates and on himself: "Why is he here? Should he be moved to general studies?", "Self-destructive tendencies," "Possible successor next to Lucifer," and a photo from last night of Frostbite pinning him to a wall. Anubis walks in. Kage claims he came for advice. Anubis calls the lie, then gives him a flat "systems" speech as a test. Kage agrees just to escape. Anubis realizes something is very wrong, and that he never leaves his door unlocked.

**Details I caught.**

- The key cast: Kage fills the lock with glue, hardens it, and pulls out "a perfect key cast." It's one of the cleverest uses of his power in the book, and he uses it again in 2.11.
- The office: "surprisingly plain… bare white… only a single Anubis action figure on one of them." In 2.9, Huoyan quotes Anubis: "I don't decorate my office because that would be a waste of money." The one decoration is his own merchandise.
- The folders: "Black Market," "Villain Encounters," "Student Prospects," sorted by year and graduating class. Anubis is building files on children.
- Last year's freshman folder: lots of Elias, Lucifer, and Reina; "a few of Ailani, some of Orion, and a couple of Abby."
- "Not showing signs of trauma" written on a photo of Elias. That's the first clue about Elias. Section 3 shows what he's traumatized by (Abaddon), and Section 4 confirms it ("You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again").
- Kage "simply took photos of them like he was told." He's handing his classmates' files to villains. The book doesn't dwell on how bad that is, and maybe it should.
- The photo of Kage asleep in Huoyan's class: "**Why is he here? Should he be moved to general studies?**" In 1.11, Kage imagined people saying "Move him to general studies." He was right.
- "It always did confuse Kage as to why he was in the top class… Maybe they pitied him?… it could look bad on Huoyan." "Top class" again, implying lower ones.
- This year's folder: the first photo is the spar, with Kage "spinning like a top." Orion: "**Good fit for the Elite Program.**" Kage: "**Kage still needs a lot of work.**" The Elite Program is planted 1,000 lines before the tournament.
- The robonoid photo: "**Self-destructive tendencies.**" Anubis knows. He does nothing about it.
- The race photo: "Exponential growth over a short period. Hero name stated to be: Sticky Situation." So the scouts were real, and they wrote the name down.
- "I'm trying to get on Anubis's payroll too." Kage half-joking.
- The Severine photo: "**Advanced problem-solving skills. Possible successor next to Lucifer.**" Kage is "a bit starstruck."
- "He once again assumed the photo came from the security cameras, but he still wondered how Anubis could get such a good angle." In 2.9, we learn Anubis has a city drone, and Nerezza destroys it in 2.6. The angles are explained, eventually.
- The last photo: Kage pinned by Frostbite's ice, last night. "**How did he survive the attack? Kage is possibly stronger than originally thought.**" Notice: the note says "survive the attack," not "meet with villains." Either the photo didn't catch Thanatos saving him, or Anubis is choosing his words carefully.
- Anubis enters with "a small clap of excitement": "You came here to ask me something, yes? Sorry I wasn't in; I was busy talking to Huoyan."
- "**Kage, that's a lie.** That is not why you collapsed." Anubis catches lies quickly. The real Anubis weighed hearts to judge truth. (See Names, Decoded.)
- "Then he lowered his sunglasses an inch, and his voice turned flatter, like he was giving a mandatory assembly speech." "Strength will grow naturally… **We have systems this way for a reason.**" Then: "He waited." It's a test. Anubis is checking whether Kage will argue.
- Kage agrees and bolts. Anubis: "I say the most teacher-y thing I've ever said in my life, and he just says yes? That answer wasn't like the data I have on Kage at all… **And not a single mention of Frostbite.**" He was waiting for Kage to report the attack.
- "**And, wait. I never leave this door unlocked.**" Anubis knows someone broke in.

**What it's doing.** It turns Anubis from a distant idol into a watcher with files on everyone, and puts Kage's two worlds on a collision course.

**What works.** The folder notes are a brilliant device. In a few lines each, they tell us what the number one hero thinks of every student, and they confirm Kage's worst fears and wildest hopes in the same scene. Anubis's test is clever, and his final line is a great button.

**What I'd push on.** The stakes of Kage photographing his classmates' files for villains are enormous, and the chapter treats it as a box to check. A single paragraph where he hesitates over Abby's photo, or Orion's, would make the betrayal felt. It would also give 2.6 another layer: Nerezza may know things about Abby because Kage gave her file away.

**Score: 8.5/10.**

## 1.15 You're My Problem

*2,396 words. The schoolyard, a few weeks later (a Thursday).*

**What happens.** Weeks pass with no word from Thanatos. Kage has gone quiet. Huoyan orders Orion to check on him, because Anubis asked and because Orion owes Anubis two punishments. Orion finds Kage in the schoolyard, blasting glue and eating a block of sharp cheddar for lipids. They argue: Kage accuses Orion of blocking him from Rhatella's gear and calls him "just like Lucifer"; Orion tells him it's his own fault he's weak, because he did nothing last year. Kage snaps, raises six cement pillars with telekinesis for the first time, seals both exits, and fights. He drops the pillars mid-air, plants compressed glue mines, loses to Orion's Zephyr counter, cries "Don't you think I'm strong..?", and then, when Orion softens, springs every mine at once. Orion is glued to the ground. Kage steps on his chest, kicks him unconscious, gently removes the glue, carries him to the nurse's office, and collapses beside him.

**Details I caught.**

- "A few weeks had passed since Kage's first 'mission.'" The scare quotes are the narrator's.
- "He acted more like he did last year again. Only in the silence aspect, however." The ghost is back, but he's training harder than ever.
- Huoyan: "Vance. Approach." Orion: "Have I done something wrong?" "You need to use me as an example in one of your lectures?" Orion is the model student and knows it.
- Huoyan: "you and Neosame are the only people he likes talking to. I would've asked her, but I've seen the way she talks to him. **Not sure if it's a crush or whatever, but she will just affirm him.** I need someone who can actually crack him open a bit." Huoyan reads his students well.
- Orion: "me and Kage aren't friends." Huoyan: "Did I ask if you were friends or claim you to be friends? No."
- "Play a video game with him or something." Huoyan cites this in 2.2 when they show up bloody.
- Orion: "Abby, Rhatella, hell, **even Ailani asked me about him once. That freaked me out.**" Ailani still cares. Orion has a reason to remember that conversation (see 2.6).
- Huoyan: "While I'm all for going against Anubis, you owe it to him considering he's got you out of not one, but two of my punishments." Orion repeats the "all for going against Anubis" line verbatim in 2.2.
- "That boy is gonna die at this rate."
- Orion uses his power in the hallway, "even though it was against the rules." He breaks a rule to get this errand over with faster.
- The schoolyard: "a bunch of grass surrounding a lone gazebo. There were doors on both sides because it was at the heart of the school. The yard had no roof." Two doors, which Kage seals.
- "Kage was shooting blasts of glue onto the ground with one hand, and had…cheese in the other." "**Eating cheese is the best way to get them into my body. I really didn't feel like drinking straight up oil. I've done it before. That shit is nasty.**" Sharp cheddar, specifically. It's a perfect detail that grows straight out of the power card's "lipids."
- Kage: "You didn't cock-block me from getting that gear from that rat lady, did you?… Y'know, you're getting to be just like Lucifer. Leave me alone, and go make out with Rhatty-whatever her name is."
- Orion: "Hey, don't talk about Rhatella that way! It's not even like that between us." He's protective of her.
- Kage: "I don't have a problem with Rhatella! I was happy to talk with her, **and I don't even know if I want that gear anymore!**" He's already drifting from the support she represents.
- "You're not even top of the class."
- Orion: "Kage, y'know, you're so in your head that you can't even see the world around you… **It's not my fault you're weaker than me; it's yours. You're the one who did nothing last year!**" That's the line that breaks him, because it's true.
- "Something in him just snapped… A smile formed on his face. His hair started to flick up."
- **The epithets.** Kage calls Orion "Diaktoros," "Khrysorrhapis," "Angelos," "Polygios," "Kyllenios," and finally "Hermes." Every one of those is a real epithet of the Greek god Hermes. *Diaktoros* means guide or messenger. *Khrysorrhapis* means "of the golden wand." *Angelos* means messenger. *Polygios* is an epithet from a cult statue at Troezen. *Kyllenios* means "of Mount Kyllene," where Hermes was born. This is the Prologue's "sharp enough to ace anything" finally showing. Kage went home after the race and studied Greek mythology to insult his rival accurately.
- Orion: "Ah, so that's why you called me Mercury before. **I am Greek, Kage, not Roman.**" He means "if you're going to compare me to a god, use the Greek one." But read cold, the line could sound like he's saying he's ethnically Greek, which is confusing with his description. One clause would fix it ("My power's Greek, Kage, not Roman").
- **First telekinesis.** "Six cement pillars… shot up around him. Then, his hair fully stood up… the others floated up." "This was the first time he'd actually been able to do telekinesis right." 2.12 explains why it worked: "Back then, he'd been sure he was better than whoever stood in front of him."
- The softened pillars become "large gray sludge blobs" sealing both exits.
- "Nowhere to run, Khrysorrhapis!"
- Kage cuts off Orion's speech: "**This isn't a movie, asshole! You don't get to monologue! If I was a villain, you'd already be dead!**" He's secretly working for villains. He's also, of course, the book's champion monologuer.
- Kage drops the pillars when Orion is on them: "Turns out we're falling, Angelos!"
- The glue mines: "I compress balls of glue so hard that they explode on contact!" "Damn, those pyrotechnics are awesome! Well, I guess it wouldn't be 'pyro', but still!"
- Orion: "I told you, Kage. Perception and timing. The timing was perfect; you had let your guard down. It did hurt to flare Zephyr Mode again like that."
- **The tears.** "This isn't fair! I deserve to win! I do, I do, I do! I'm strong now…! I've been working so hard; I have to be! **D-Don't you think I'm strong..?**" Then "a single laugh," and the mines converge. Were the tears real? I think they were, and that's what makes it awful and brilliant: he weaponized a real breakdown. Orion "felt a flicker of sorrow" and stepped forward, and that kindness is exactly what lost him the fight.
- The telekinetic mines are the move Rebento uses in 4.5 ("he did that move myself"), which is how Kage knows Orion has to get low.
- "Only his face was visible."
- Kage steps on Orion's chest, the way Severine stood on his: "**You let your guard down, Kyllenios.** Have a good rest." He returns Orion's own phrase to him. Then he kicks Orion in the temple.
- And then the tenderness: "Kage's hair stood on end again. This time, the glue slowly slid off Orion." He carries Orion to the nurse. "**It's always fun going up against you, Hermes…**" After five insults, the sixth name is affectionate.
- "When they arrived, Kage collapsed in front of it and lay unconscious next to Orion." Just like the end of 1.2, both boys down together.

**What it's doing.** It ends Section 1 with Kage's first win over Orion, the first telekinesis, and the first time Kage's cruelty and tenderness show up in the same breath.

**What works.** Almost everything. It's the best fight in Section 1: inventive, emotional, rule-based. The epithets are a gift for careful readers. The ending mirrors 1.2 exactly (both boys unconscious together) and shows how far they've come.

**What I'd push on.** The temple kick is brutal, and the book moves past it fast. Kage kicks an incapacitated opponent in the head. That's the first sign of the cruelty the biomass will later amplify, and it happens *before* the biomass. That's an important thing for readers to notice: the darkness was already there. A beat of Kage seeing what he did, or of Orion mentioning it in 2.2, would underline it.

**Score: 8.5/10.**

## Section 1 overall

Section 1 is named for Orion, and it's the story of Kage finding something to push against. It moves from a boy hitting a bag alone to a boy carrying his rival to the nurse's office. Along the way, it plants nearly everything the rest of the book needs: the whistle, the maid photo, "another speedster," "eat breakfast," the name, the name speech, the King speech, the files, the villains, Frostbite's hand. The weakest stretch is 1.7 to 1.8, which has to introduce too many people at once. The strongest is 1.11 to 1.15, which is as good a five-chapter run as anything in the book.

**Section score: 8.3/10.**


# Section 2: "Abby Neosame"

## 2.1 Patient Confidentiality

*1,589 words. Flashback: the second week of Abby's first year.*

**What happens.** Brand-new first-year Abby hunts down Lucifer, alone, because her power has shown her that something is draining his energy from inside. He thinks she's blackmailing him and threatens to burn her alive. She's confused: she just wants to help, and nurses keep patient confidentiality. He says it's his enhancement and can't be healed, and makes her promise three times not to tell. Then she asks him why he's so mean. He admits he turns up his worst traits so people stay away, because more connections mean more chances someone sees his secret. He's only nice to Elias ("history") and Reina (he blushes). Abby leaves him alone, because that's what helps him. "I'm happy to not be your friend, Lucifer."

**Details I caught.**

- The section is named for Abby, and it opens with a flashback where she's the protagonist. Section 4 is named for Ailani, and it opens (after Chapter NULL) with a flashback where Ailani is the center. The two sections named for girls both begin by showing who that girl was before the present tests her. It's a strong structural rhyme.
- "She was going to be able to help people as a job!" Pure.
- "She had her first target of assistance already." "Target" is a funny word for a patient.
- Lucifer: "Just cuz you know my name doesn't mean I want to talk to you… I'm not in the mood to be all personable." Even at fourteen or fifteen, he's built walls.
- Abby does "a prayer motion."
- "I'm so bored waiting for Elias and Reina here. I'm gonna beat them both for making me wait so long." Lucifer is always waiting on those two.
- "**I can tell something is zapping your energy from inside!**" Abby diagnoses Abaddon in her second week of school. It takes the rest of the book 1,500 lines to explain what she saw.
- "I was going to tell Huoyan to help you, but I thought it might be better if I talked to you first." She chose to respect him over reporting him.
- Lucifer's flame: "You came here to threaten me?! **Oh, of course there'd be some freak in my class who can see right through me.**" Then the stock threat: "I will burn you alive!" He says it to Kage too. He never does it.
- Abby: "Why would you want to burn me alive? I just want to make you feel better." She doesn't understand the threat, and that disarms him.
- "I know nothing is free. What's your angle?" Lucifer believes everything is transactional. In 3.8, he proves it by negotiating with Anubis: "What's in it for me?"
- "**Nurses and doctors have patient confidentiality!**" The title, and the reason Abby never tells anyone. She keeps that promise until she dies.
- "Sorry, you can't heal what's wrong with me. It's…something with my enhancement."
- "Is there anybody else that has some power that lets you see me?" "No, I'm the only one! Ooo, there is a girl who can see stuff when she wraps her hair around you!" That's Zulfiya, and it's why her hair matters in 3.4.
- "**Enhancements shouldn't feed on their users.**" Abby states a rule of the world. Abaddon violates it.
- "She'd been told to wait before, and she didn't want anything bad to happen to Lucifer." That's a seed: 2.5 reveals she was told to wait while her brother died.
- "Now, for the third time, say you won't tell anyone." She promises.
- "**Why are you so mean?**" Lucifer: "You that blunt or are you just trying to fuck with me?" Abby: "I wouldn't call it blunt! I'm just honest!"
- "I'd be happy to show you some activities that I do when I feel mean." Adorable.
- **Lucifer's thesis:** "I…turn up my poor traits to get people to not want to talk to me. If I make more connections, then more people have a chance to see what's going on with me. That's a liability I can't have. So, I crank my intensity up to eleven." This reframes every cruel thing Lucifer does in the book. It doesn't excuse it, but it explains it.
- "Me and Elias have history. And Reina, she…uhm…" A blush. "DON'T WORRY ABOUT WHY I TALK TO REINA!"
- "You after money?" "I was gifted with the power to heal others! **God chose me** to be the one who makes sure everybody's okay! I don't know why you were gifted with fire, but I'm sure there's some divine purpose you'll find!" An angel telling a demon he has a divine purpose.
- "Since I got here last week, everyone seems to have some desire. You're telling me you've got nothing?" "**Is helping others not a desire?**" Abby's lack of a personal dream is a real question the book asks. She tells Kage in 1.5 she hopes "to find her own reason."
- Lucifer: "**go help someone else.** I don't need your help, Abby." That's the title of 2.5.
- "I'll leave you alone because you said that being alone would be the best way to help you."
- Reina waves "next to the stoic Elias."
- Lucifer doesn't wave goodbye "because he didn't want anyone thinking there was anything going on. Abby understood that."
- "**I'm happy to not be your friend, Lucifer.**" The "we're not friends" motif, inverted. For Kage and Orion, "not friends" hides affection. For Abby, *staying* not-friends is the affection.

**What it's doing.** It recontextualizes Lucifer before Section 2 makes him a villain, establishes Abby's ethics (help, consent, confidentiality), and plants Abaddon and Abby's brother.

**What works.** It's a perfect two-hander. Abby's innocence keeps pulling the truth out of Lucifer without her trying. It also quietly rewrites 1.7: "Abby's eyes tracked Lucifer like a hawk. Her smile slipped." She wasn't glaring. She was watching his condition.

**What I'd push on.** The chapter is placed right after the cliffhanger of 1.15, which delays the aftermath of the schoolyard fight. That's a fine choice, but the jump back to Year 1 would land more cleanly with a one-line marker, even just "A year and a half earlier."

**Score: 8.5/10.**

## 2.2 No More Fighting

*1,945 words. The nurse's office, Friday.*

**What happens.** Kage wakes up with Zulfiya's hair wrapped around his IV arm this time, so he can't rip it out. Abby is healing Orion. Huoyan walks in smiling, slams both boys' heads into the wall, reveals he'd secretly hoped they could reach Lucifer and Elias's level together, and hands down the punishment: they're banned from fighting each other (suspension, then expulsion), and they're doing night patrols together starting Monday. Zulfiya reveals Kage's temperature hit 110°F. As Kage leaves, Abby gives him a gold whistle her late brother made, which only she can hear. "If you ever need support or help from me, blow it!"

**Details I caught.**

- Kage wakes "suddenly," "hyperventilating." He wakes this way again after the rebuild (2.7) and Lucifer wakes this way in his mindscape (3.16).
- "Morning, King." Zulfiya.
- "the IV wasn't in the same arm as last time… It was totally wrapped up in Zulfiya's hair." "Sorry, your majesty. **I had to make sure you wouldn't get away this time.**" She learned from 1.11. It's a funny, consistent detail.
- Abby: "I just came by as quick as I could when I heard it was you and Orion that went down!"
- Orion's injuries: "bruises all over his body and his muscles were strained like crazy! His joints were also in rough shape!" Zephyr Mode's cost, exactly as his card said.
- Kage: "We're really stupid people." Orion: "Speak for yourself! I am not even a quarter as dumb as you are! Plus, it's not like I forgot how you won! You tricked me!" Kage: "It doesn't matter how I won. I still beat you." That's his whole ethic in seven words, and Lance challenges it in Section 3.
- "Oh, my sovereign!" Zulfiya's royal titles keep rotating: King, your highness, your majesty, sovereign.
- Huoyan walks in "with something very terrifying. / A smile." The paragraph break is the joke.
- The girls flee to opposite corners. Huoyan pats both heads, then slams them into the wall and "facepalmed maybe a million times."
- "I told you that if you needed to compete with him, play a video game. Instead, you punch him in the face?!"
- Orion, monotone: "**Sir, respectfully, I told you something like this would happen.**" One of Orion's funniest lines.
- Huoyan's "normal pale skin was turning a dark shade of angry red." Kage laughs. Huoyan shoots a fireball at his chest. That burns him, which matters: in 3.15, Lucifer's fire doesn't seem to, and Huoyan notices.
- **Huoyan's confession:** "I watched you two in front of Anubis, and I thought, 'Wow, maybe they could get to Tulivalo and Minkowski's level. Vance has been great, and if Vance is pushing Ozul, those two could go so far together!' I was so excited underneath all this trepidation. I stayed cool to see how you guys progress." The cold teacher was hoping. It's the first time we see his real heart.
- "Normally, I'd have you both write me reports until both of your fingers bleed, but that wouldn't work for either of you. Ozul just wouldn't do it, and Vance's hands move too fast." Great line.
- The ban. The patrols. "You start Monday! Enjoy your weekend; it's the last weekend you'll be able to sleep!"
- Orion: "I never want to agree with Kage about anything, but if I am, you can guarantee that what he's saying is correct."
- Orion: "When I spoke to Huoyan yesterday, he said he's '**all for going against Anubis**.'" A verbatim callback to 1.15. It also dates the schoolyard fight to Thursday.
- "Kage actually ripped a tuft of his hair out."
- "HOW ARE ME AND YOU SUPPOSED TO NOT FIGHT?! IT'S WHAT WE DO!"
- "I gotta work with Mr. Perception-And-Timing like he's my friend now?"
- Abby: "**This is the first time we've talked together since you guys' first fight!** It's been fun seeing you guys talk!" She finally got her wish from 1.7.
- Kage slips: "go back to healing **Hermes**." Abby: "Hermes..?" The nickname is now something he says without thinking. Zulfiya hears it, which matters in 2.14.
- Zulfiya: "**King, you were one hundred and ten degrees when I started healing you.**" The fever ladder: 102 (1.2), 105 (1.2), 110 (here). "That's a new record! Nice, I'm breaking limits, baby!" "**My body is probably built for that, anyway!** I do have a power that causes heat from it, so maybe my DNA is meant for that." No human survives 110°F without damage. This is another clue that Kage's body is unusual.
- "It seemed the care was genuine, so that made Kage a little anxious." He doesn't know what to do with being cared for.
- "Please don't end up here again, King? I think Huoyan might kill you if you do."
- **The whistle.** "My brother made this whistle before he died. I'm not exactly sure how it works, but I know only I can hear it! If you ever need support or help from me, blow it! I'll come as quickly as I can!" "He noticed that familiar golden glitter from the training room." That pays off the sparkle in 1.5. It's also the biggest loaded gun in the book.
- Kage: "Thanks. **I'll be sure to use it a ton.**" He doesn't use it once until she's dead.
- Zulfiya: "You should be calling him King like me!" Abby, blushing: "What??? No! That's weird! He's Kage!"
- Orion: "I can't get away from the guy… **At this rate, I'm gonna be the one to kill him.**" That sentence haunts Orion in 2.8.

**What it's doing.** It turns the rivalry into a forced partnership, hands Kage the whistle, and sets the patrol in motion.

**What works.** Huoyan's scene is his best so far: physical comedy, real anger, and then a confession that reframes him. The whistle handoff is quiet and devastating in hindsight. Orion's line at the end is perfect dramatic irony.

**What I'd push on.** Huoyan slams two students' heads into a wall and shoots a fireball at one. It's played for comedy, and the book's tone can carry it, but it's worth knowing that some readers will wince. The book later treats Anubis as the "bad" adult and Huoyan as the "good" one, so it might help to have Huoyan's physical discipline acknowledged somewhere (a student joking about it, or Huoyan regretting the fireball after seeing Kage heal).

**Score: 7.5/10.**

## 2.3 Arts And Crafts

*1,801 words. Training room two's hidden mirror room, the same day.*

**What happens.** Kage wanders into training room two and finds a hidden mirror room where Ailani is dancing. They argue loudly (both have been avoiding room one to avoid each other), escalate to a standoff (sigil versus dripping glue), and stand down together. They sit. She admits she hears about him from everyone. He apologizes for his scene at the start of the year. She agrees to talk again, "We aren't back together yet." "You said 'yet'!" She shows him a crystal butterfly that flaps its wings. He tries a glue butterfly; it collapses. She invites him to train with her. He refuses, because he always ends up fighting people he trains with, and he doesn't want to fight her.

**Details I caught.**

- Kage still hasn't heard from Thanatos: "None of his messages even showed as read." "Did I not do enough? No, no. I got them a ton of photos. It's probably fine; I just need to keep staying patient." That's a foster kid's anxiety about being sent away for not being good enough.
- The room: "a room full of mirrors, clearly made for yoga and ballet." A small pink speaker. Ailani is dancing. This is the first clue to her dream (choreographer and dancer, revealed in 4.1).
- Mirrors. The Prologue ends with Kage looking at his reflection. Their reconciliation happens in a room made of reflections.
- The argument is pure farce: "I haven't gone there since you saw me on the treadmill!" "I haven't gone there since that day either!" Both have been dodging room one for months to avoid each other.
- "Not to be rude, but if I wanted to see you, I would've sat next to you in the SAME CLASS WE HAVE!" Confirms they share a class.
- "Dull tools only get duller without someone like me to sharpen them!" "Or, hell, maybe I found someone else to sharpen me!" He means Thanatos. She doesn't know. Dramatic irony.
- "Ooooo, don't make me slap you **again**!" She's slapped him before. She'll slap him again in 2.14, and Orion in 2.14 and 3.5.
- "Crystal girl is gonna shoot her demure crystals at me?" That's how we learn her power is crystals.
- "You're lucky I haven't **crystalled** you to the wall already!" She verbs it.
- The sigil: "He recognized that sign. That meant her enhancement was active, and that also meant it was a threat." He knows her tells. They used to be close.
- "I see that sigil disappear, and you'll see my nails stop spewing…" They de-escalate together, at the same moment. That's a relationship in one beat.
- "You match me pretty well, Ms. Shoma."
- "freshman class's lazy bum" / "Hey! We're sophomores now!"
- **What Ailani has heard:** "Huoyan mentions you on occasion, Orion talks about you sometimes, Rhatella's sister mentions you because apparently Rhatella never shuts up about you, Zulfiya has mentioned you as…king, and Abby calls you her friend." Rhatella "never shuts up" about Kage. That's the first hint of her crush.
- Rhatella's sister: "She's the big girl in our class. I'm friends with her." That's Geneki, set up 800 lines before we meet her. And it's why Ailani rewatches Geneki's loss so many times that she uses it to beat Lance in 4.7.
- "I am not mean! You made me act that way with that whole scene you made at the start of the year." Fair. He apologizes: "Yeah, I guess I did. Sorry about that."
- "Fine. We can talk again, but that's it! **We aren't back together yet**, so don't go acting like we are!"
- "You said 'yet'!" eight times, jumping. The repetition is the joke and it's earned.
- "You only started training, and that's it. Other than that, you're the exact same idiot." She's right, and the next chapter proves it.
- **The butterfly.** "Ailani's hands showed sigils, and she squeezed her hands together. When she opened them up, a small butterfly made of crystal was in her left hand. She cutely poked it with her index finger, **the sigil on her hand turned ninety degrees**, and the butterfly floated upward." The rotating sigil is her telekinesis mechanic. She uses it again in 4.7 to float a crystal platform back into the ring. That's continuity across 3,000 lines.
- A butterfly is a symbol of transformation. Abaddon, in 3.16, calls Kage "a bug that needs to be squashed before he cocoons and grows wings!"
- Kage's glue butterfly "deformed and soon fell to the ground in a glue splat. He stared at the splat for a while. He didn't understand how he was struggling to do telekinesis after his fight with Orion." In 2.12, the narration explains: "Somewhere around the mirror room, he'd stopped being sure." Telekinesis runs on certainty, and in front of Ailani, he isn't certain of anything.
- "Something to work toward is always good, right?"
- "When would you even have time to do more practice?" "I'll find time, or I'll make time."
- Ailani: "You could always train with me. I don't really mind all that much." Kage: "**I don't really train too well with others. I end up fighting them almost always, and to be honest, I don't want to fight you.**… I'd be pretty mad at myself if I did end up hurting you." First-year Kage, in 4.1, says almost the same thing after she flattens him: "I don't want to hurt you." This is consistent in him, and it's the setup for the fight readers now expect.
- "I'm not a porcelain doll, Kage." "If we fought, I'd kick your ass, Kage!"
- He leaves, smiling, and slams "the max settings button with a smile."

**What it's doing.** It reopens the Kage–Ailani door, establishes her power and her tell, and quietly explains why his telekinesis breaks.

**What works.** The argument is genuinely funny, and the de-escalation is genuinely tender. The butterfly is the right object. The "yet" bit is perfect.

**What I'd push on.** The chapter would land even harder if Kage noticed, for one line, that Ailani was dancing, and remembered something about it. The book saves her dream for 4.1, which is fine, but a small "he'd forgotten she danced" would plant it with feeling instead of just information.

**Score: 8/10.**

## 2.4 That Didn't Last Long

*1,372 words. Ailani's dorm, then Lucifer's, the following Monday.*

**What happens.** Saturday is great, Sunday goes cold, and Monday Ailani accuses Kage of flirting with other women. Lucifer told her about Reina. Kage admits he did it, but before the weekend. Ailani quotes his promise from 1.3 back to him word for word and asks whether the Reina flirt came before or after it. After. "Lose my number." Kage storms down the hall to Lucifer's door. Lucifer mocks him (Elias is inside), and Kage nearly starts a fight he can't win. Orion arrives with a whistle (a real one, not Abby's) to collect Kage for their first patrol. Kage whispers that they could take both of them together. Orion says no and covers Kage's mouth. Elias: "I pray you guys come back okay."

**Details I caught.**

- "He talked to Ailani all of Saturday, but Sunday was odd." Two days of happiness.
- "That looks like the face I make when doing Huoyan's homework!"
- "I thought you had changed, but no, of course not. **Silly me for having hope.**"
- Lucifer "said that you were flirting with Reina. Hearing it from him was weird, too. It was one of the few times he ever spoke to me."
- Kage: "I did flirt with Reina! But, I didn't do it this weekend! I did it…before…"
- Ailani: "Before you said '**...none of those other ladies could even come close to you. You're the one I wanna be with, and you always have been.**' Was it before that?" That's a verbatim quote of L104. She remembered every word. That's devastating, because it means his promise mattered to her.
- Kage answers honestly: "No, Ailani. It was after I had said that." He doesn't lie to her. He lies to almost everyone else in this book, but not to her, here.
- "**And, Kage, lose my number.**" In 2.14: "Keep my number."
- He "banged on the door like he was a police officer."
- Lucifer's tail "began swinging happily." The tail is a mood ring.
- "Hey, Min! The ant decided to show up today! **Maybe Anubis sent him on a mission to find us!**" Kage *has* been on a mission, for villains. Lucifer doesn't know how close he is.
- "What do you need, **period hair**?" A crude red-hair insult. "Did daddy Orion say that you should come here and see how real heroes operate?"
- Lucifer's insult list in this scene alone: ant, period hair, worm, pebble, little Ozul. Small creatures and small things.
- Kage holds back "because Elias was there, he didn't want to take the risk of getting attacked by the two top students." He's learned some caution.
- Lucifer: "she deserves transparency, you get me?" "Actions have consequences." "**Brown-nosing Anubis isn't helping either.**" Lucifer's real grievance.
- Lucifer pats Kage's head: "Too bad, pebble." The head pat as domination.
- Elias appears: "Is there an issue, Luci? Do you need some help with this?" "Affirmative."
- "A whistle was heard from the end of the hallway." Orion whistles. The first whistle sound in the book is Orion's, and it's a rescue.
- "Our first night mission is tonight! Don't tell me you forgot?"
- Lucifer's tail "stopped and stiffened." Orion is "only an inch taller, but that inch pissed Lucifer off pretty strongly."
- Kage whispers: "**If we work together, I think we can take both of them out easily.**" A week ago, Kage wanted to "handle it all on my own." Now his first instinct is a team-up with Orion. That's growth, even if it's growth toward violence.
- Orion: "It's idiotic to the highest degree, and it isn't a risk I'm willing to take. It's a scuffle for no reason, anyhow."
- Orion "quickly covered his mouth."
- Elias: "**I pray you guys come back okay. I would love to continue this conversation together.**" "His words came out ominous, yet they sounded genuine." In 2.11, Kage remembers this line: "It was like he knew something bad was going to happen." Is Elias precognitive? His power is about time. (See Theories.)
- Lucifer: "Don't forget to tell Anubis I say hi next time you see him! I'm sure he'd love to have his little scouts let him know how the top dogs of the class are doing!" Lucifer knows about the scouts.

**What it's doing.** It breaks the Kage–Ailani reconciliation, cements Lucifer as Kage's social enemy, and sends Kage and Orion out the door.

**What works.** Ailani's quote is the best moment: a callback that's also a character beat, because it shows how closely she was listening. The Lucifer confrontation is tense, and Orion's rescue sets up the team.

**What I'd push on.** How did Lucifer know Kage and Ailani were talking again? They reconciled on a Friday in a hidden room, and by Monday he'd reported to her. Either give him a source (Rhatella's sister? Zulfiya? he overheard Ailani on the phone?), or plant it as a mystery. Right now it reads like a hole.

**Score: 7/10.**

## 2.5 Go Help Someone Else

*886 words. Lucifer's doorway, the same Monday.*

**What happens.** As Kage and Orion leave, Abby catches Lucifer at his door. The thing draining him has gotten worse. She begs to try her power. He screams at her, calls her "a nothing support hero," and tells her to go bother Kage instead. He kicks her foot out of the doorway and slams the door. Abby curls up and cries, and we learn why she can't wait: her brother was sick, doctors said she was too young, she listened, and he died. She decides to help Kage on patrol tonight. Inside, Elias says Abby might be right. Lucifer: "I don't want her hanging around and ending up hurt!… Angels can't befriend demons."

**Details I caught.**

- The shortest chapter in the book (886 words), and one of the most important.
- Lucifer to Elias: "If I was able to get my hands on Kage again, I don't think I could stop myself from killing him." Elias, "deadpan": "**Don't kill anyone while I sleep.**" That line becomes much darker when we learn that Elias guards Lucifer's sleep.
- Elias is "heading to lay back down." In Lucifer's room. (In 2.11 Kage discovers Lucifer's dorm has only one bedroom.)
- "He readied himself to bash whoever was putting their hands on him, only to turn around and see a halo." That's a great sentence.
- "Scanning your body right now, I can see that the thing that was draining you is getting even worse!"
- "Well, then stop scanning me!" "**I can't! My power scans on its own! Every day I have to see you hurting…**" Abby's power is a burden. She can't stop seeing pain.
- "Abby stuck her foot in the way. **Her tiny leg trembled before the tall devil in front of her, but she stood her ground.**" In 2.8, Orion puts his foot in Ailani's door the same way.
- "We had this exact same conversation last year. What has changed?!"
- "I don't need you, I don't want you, and I especially don't need your pity! Just cuz you think your life's purpose is to help people does not mean **we—does not mean I** require it!" That slip, "we," is important. He corrects himself. Who's "we"? Lucifer and Abaddon? Lucifer and Elias? Abby's next words point at Elias.
- "I'm not your friend! I'm not even your acquaintance! I'm just the dude at the top, and **you're a nothing support hero! The best doesn't need support!**" The cruelest line he says to anyone in the book. And Lucifer, of all people, needs support every night.
- Abby: "But, what about Elias..?" Lucifer: "STOP ASKING ABOUT HIM!" What did she see in Elias? It's never answered. (Trauma? Exhaustion from keeping watch? Something in his body?)
- "What happened to you and that bug Kage?! BOTHER HIM!" He sends her to her death without knowing it.
- He kicks her foot away and slams the door.
- **Abby's backstory:** "Her brother was incredibly sick, but Abby was too young for doctors to trust her. At the time, Abby listened… **But she never tried, and that's what's been killing her.** There might have been a chance that if she went against their orders, if she listened to her heart instead of what everyone was saying, her brother might still be here." That's why she couldn't wait for Lucifer, and why she won't wait for Kage to blow the whistle. And it's the same wound as Kage's: a death she thinks she could have prevented. The two of them were the same person, and neither knew.
- "A part of her thought that everything with them may be fine since Kage hadn't blown the whistle, **but another part thought that maybe Kage would never blow it.**" She knows him. Orion says the same thing in 2.6 after she dies.
- "Fine. I'll help you later, Lucifer… I'll just go help Kage today." Her last decision, made out of hurt.
- Elias: "She might be right, Luci. You have been more tired than usual."
- Lucifer: "**Whether she's right or wrong doesn't matter, Min! I don't want her hanging around and ending up hurt!**" His cruelty is protection. He's afraid Abaddon will hurt her. And she gets hurt anyway, because he sent her to Kage.
- "You make an exception for Reina." "Reina's different and you know that. **Our enhancements work perfectly together.**" In 3.14, we learn why: Reina absorbs heat. She's the only person who can survive his fire.
- "**At the end of the day, angels can't befriend demons.**"

**What it's doing.** It's the trigger. It sends Abby to her death, gives her a wound that mirrors Kage's, and makes Lucifer accidentally responsible.

**What works.** Everything. It's short, it's brutal, and every line points somewhere. The brother backstory arrives at exactly the right moment, right before she dies, so her death becomes a choice made in his memory.

**What I'd push on.** Nothing major. If anything, I'd make sure Lucifer, at some point, connects "go bother Kage" to what happened. When he asks "whether Abby was with them" in 2.8, with his tail stiff, he's already wondering. A scene where he realizes he sent her would be a gut-punch for his arc.

**Score: 9/10.**

## 2.6 Divine Intervention

*4,073 words. City streets, Monday night. The longest chapter in the book.*

**What happens.** On patrol, Kage complains about Lucifer and the ban, and Orion lets slip that Ailani has asked about Kage (and blushes). A scream: a government official has been murdered and mangled, and a masked woman on a roof is smashing a strange camera. Orion calls Kage "Sticky" for anonymity; Kage names Orion "Hermes." The villain, Nerezza, crushes the corpse's skull and recognizes Kage from the alley. Their first team move fails (they collide). Nerezza's shadow tentacles nearly kill them. Kage despairs. Orion yells, "This isn't the Sticky Situation I've met!" and they land a hit. Then Nerezza shreds Orion. Kage, afraid she'll expose his villain ties, won't call Huoyan, Anubis, or Abby; he texts Thanatos. Abby arrives anyway, freezes, and is targeted. Orion breaks 300 mph to save her, and Nerezza stabs her from her own shadow. Impaled, Abby smiles at Kage: "It's okay, Kage…everything is going to be alright… I will always be your number one supporter." She detonates herself in a blast of light called Divine Judgement. All that's left is her scorched dolphin backpack. Nerezza survives and attacks Kage, and Thanatos catches the tentacle: "I told you I was done saving you."

**Details I caught.**

- Kage "stretching… his shirt. It was on the verge of ripping." His frustration in one gesture.
- "Villains should not hear us coming." Kage: "That sneak attack shit is for villains!… **Attacking from behind is so pussy…**" He sneak-attacked Orion with tape in 1.6 and mined him in 1.15. He's working for villains. The hypocrisy is total, and he can't see it.
- "He did it to fuck with me! I don't get why he is always trying to throw me off my game! Is he THAT threatened by me?"
- Orion: "I think he just knows Anubis has eyes." "You're not exactly…muscular, Kage." Kage: "I'm just lean and not bulky like you guys."
- Orion: "**Ailani has asked me about you previously… I was pretty excited when she asked to speak to me during off hours, but then, she asked about you. Not fun.**" "The faintest blush appeared on his face. Lucky for Orion, Kage didn't notice." Orion likes Ailani. This is the love triangle's first clear sign.
- "Does your broken power do that too?!" A callback to "cheat code" (1.6).
- "My goals…" Kage "hadn't really set any goalposts. His current goal was being the number one hero." He doesn't know what he wants, besides to be first.
- The scene: "mass hysteria… a mangled corpse… right outside of a government building… in a suit… some sort of official." In 4.0, we learn the official was pushing the "Security Accords," which would put cameras all over the hero city.
- "On the roof of the building was a figure dressed in all black. A mask covered their face, and Orion could see them destroying a strange camera." That's Anubis's city drone (2.9). The villains blinded the watcher before they killed the official. Everything that happens next happens off camera.
- Orion: "**Sticky**, we should use our hero names to keep some form of anonymity." He swore in 1.6 he'd never say it. He said "That's definitely getting shortened." He shortened it.
- "You never told me your hero name, dipshit!" "Oh, I haven't, have I? It's…" "We don't have time for this! **I'm just gonna call you Hermes!**" "Hermes…works." Orion's real hero name is still unknown. In 3.6, he adopts "Hermes" publicly.
- Nerezza: "What is this? **The Z team?** A bunch of nobodies came to stop me?" She stomps the skull "like a grape."
- "One that was so clear and gross that it could be seen through the mask." Her smile shows through the mask. That's a recurring image.
- "A smile creepily crawled on her face again. **She recognized the boy. She saw him with Thanatos and Frostbite in that alleyway.**" Confirms the "other presences" in 1.13.
- "I'll go right, and you go left!" Kage throws his shoes and socks off and puts "a thin layer of adhesive on his hands and feet to cling and move on the wall." They collide. "WHAT HAPPENED TO ALL THAT 'perception and timing' BULLSHIT YOU ALWAYS SPEW!" Orion: "**we haven't exactly practiced working together. Team moves are bound to fail.**" Kage wanted "team moves" in 1.6. Orion said they didn't need any. This is the cost.
- Nerezza's card (flat villain register): **Umbrakinesis.** Shadows as parts of her body. Sees in the dark, moves silently. "Her surgical skill makes every attack a choice between mercy and pain." Limits: "She can only go twenty miles from where she has walked; beyond that, she is stuck. Too much use blinds her eyes, makes her hands feel locked, and fills her mind with pain. Too many jumps, and even the shadows deny entry." The card ends: "**She is the darkness that has taken on a human form.**" Her name means "blackness" in Italian.
- "My, you are something special!… **I see why Thanatos likes you.**" The choking stops mattering to Kage. His secret does.
- "**We're going to die here…**" No quip. No smile. The book has made us so used to Kage's jokes that his silence is terrifying.
- "Instead of moping along with him, **Orion took a page out of Kage's book.**" "WHAT ARE YOU DOING OUT THERE, STICKY?!… **This isn't the Sticky Situation I've met!** Wake up! We're losing for two reasons! One, you want to sit here and accept defeat as the fight just started, and two, you're teaming with me wrong! **You improvise, I execute!**" Orion says the full name, in a crisis, to save them. "No way in a million years" lasted about two months.
- Nerezza: "Aww! How inspiring! And, Sticky Situation is such a cute name! The newspapers will definitely headline that one! '**Sticky Situation! No longer bringing smiles!**'" She attacks the exact thing his name is for (1.9). Whether she knows it or not, it's the cruelest possible joke.
- One drop of glue gets past her shadow wall onto her face: "**How dare you ruin my beautiful face with your disgusting fluid?!** I could rip you to pieces in a moment! The only reason you're still alive is because-!" Because Thanatos wants him. Her vanity is her weakness.
- Orion's knee to her sternum sends her through a window. Kage hugs him and kisses his cheek over and over. Orion: "**Whoa, absolutely not. No kissing. I do not swing that way, Kage.**" "**Bold of you to assume I do.**"
- Kage starts to call Huoyan, and a tentacle throws him into a wall. They "always assume if they land one attack everything is over."
- "I'm not sure if I can kill you yet, but I can torture you all I want. And, killing 'Hermes' over here is well within my freedom to do."
- "The tips were sharp, and the suction cups had teeth." Octopus horror.
- **Kage's choice:** "Kage's first instinct was to call Huoyan or Anubis, but he didn't want Nerezza to possibly reveal that he had that 'special internship.'… **His other hand brushed the whistle in his pocket. Abby could help, but no. He didn't want that risk.**… He sent Thanatos their location and prayed he would arrive." Kage chooses the villain over his teachers and his supporter, to protect his secret. It's the moral low point of the book so far, and it costs Abby her life.
- Abby arrives: "Kage! Orion!" with a wave. "She had been walking through the streets of the city all night. She was calling both of their names to try and find them." She came without being called. The narration makes it plain: no whistle.
- "Her brain started overloading with all the abrasions on Orion and deep lesions on Kage's neck. **She couldn't move, frozen with fear and worry.**" Abby, who couldn't bear to wait, freezes. Her power, which shows her every wound, is what paralyzes her.
- "ABBY! GET OUT OF HERE!" "Abby…r-run…"
- Nerezza: "I need to give you guys a proper receipt from that stunt you pulled. How about this, I don't take the receipt from you, but I take it out on her?"
- She lets Orion go on purpose: "Run, boy." She wants to watch him try.
- **Orion breaks his limit:** "One-fifty, two hundred, two-fifty. Then he broke through and went even faster. A blazing comet moving at three hundred miles per hour." His card capped him at 200. He outruns the tentacle, stomps it, and gives Abby a thumbs up.
- "Oh, well done! A real hero!… **woops! My finger slipped!**" The tentacle comes from **Abby's own shadow**. Orion did everything right and it didn't matter. The villain chose the angle he couldn't cover.
- "Abby didn't even scream." "The wound was fatal. Abby was incapable of healing herself, only others. **However, she could see her own damage. She witnessed her heartbeat quicken and attempt to pump blood in the failing organs.**" The cruelest use of her power: she watches herself die in detail.
- "**Abby's wings, which Kage had never seen before,** began to flap instinctively and frantically in an attempt to fly away." He sees her wings for the first time as she's dying.
- Nerezza: "Look at your friend! You see what you cause! **These are the consequences of your actions, Kage!** Would a hero let something like this happen?! No, they wouldn't, Kage. They would've traded their life to save this poor girl. Such a sorry excuse for a hero." And she's right, because Kage didn't call for help.
- "**It's okay, Kage…everything is going to be alright. I came here to support you.**" "They were the same words she'd said the day they met, holding his split knuckles." The narration names the callback. It doesn't need to, but it's so moving it gets away with it.
- "Abby's eyes suddenly started to glow a deep yellow." "**I will fulfill that promise. I will always be your number one supporter, Kage.**"
- "The shining light loosened Nerezza's grip on Kage as she covered her eyes." The light hurts Nerezza. Kage remembers this in 2.7 and uses moonlight against her.
- Kage "ran to tackle Orion to the ground. He braced the two of them." Even now, he protects Orion.
- **Divine Judgement:** "One where she would strike down enemies as well as herself." It's the only use of the technique in the book.
- "In front of the boys lay a tattered dolphin backpack. One of the straps was torn off, and the backpack itself was singed and scorched from the blast. Kage grabbed the bag and hugged it."
- Orion: "**We won, Kage. Get up…**" The emptiest victory in the book.
- Kage: "Why was she here? I don't understand! She shouldn't have been here! This was our punishment!"
- "**Then, why did she give me this whistle?!**" Orion: "Maybe it's because she knows you barely accept help from anybody? **She might've thought you'd never use it.** You do hate asking for assistance." That's exactly what Abby thought in 2.5. Orion saw it too.
- Kage "slung the single strap over his arm and wore it." He carries her backpack from here on.
- Nerezza's back: "**I can't believe she couldn't just die gracefully! She had to blow herself up!** Someone is paying for that! And, I chose you!"
- Thanatos catches the tentacle bare-handed. "**I can't believe you're making me do this, child. I told you I was done saving you.**"

**What it's doing.** It kills the book's heart and makes Kage complicit. It's the hinge of the whole story.

**What works.** Everything, and I mean that. The chapter builds hope (the first successful team hit, Orion's "This isn't the Sticky Situation I've met," Orion breaking 300 mph) and then takes it away in the cruelest possible way: from Abby's own shadow, right after the save. Abby's death uses every rule set up about her: she can't heal herself, she sees all damage, she's a supporter, she keeps her promises, her first words were "everything is going to be alright." And the choice that kills her is Kage's. He had a whistle in his pocket and a phone in his hand, and he called a villain. The book doesn't let him off.

**What I'd push on.** Almost nothing. Two small things. First, the narration says "They were the same words she'd said the day they met." That callback is strong enough that some readers would feel it more if it weren't pointed out. Try it without that sentence and see. Second, Divine Judgement arrives as a technique name with no prior mention. You could plant it earlier, maybe as one line in her power card ("Not every ability of Abby is known") pointing at a last resort. It's already sort of there. Making it slightly more visible would let the reader dread it.

**Score: 10/10.**

## 2.7 Fractured Brought Whole Again

*4,016 words. The road out of the city, the same night.*

**What happens.** Orion recognizes Shifting Horror. Kage slaps him to keep him from thinking too hard, glues himself to Orion's back, and they flee at 100 mph. Nerezza has walled the road twenty miles out. Kage launches them upward; the wall becomes a dome; Kage takes the fall under Orion to protect his legs and cracks his ribs and shoulder blade. Nerezza drags Kage out and delivers him to Thanatos like a gift. Inside the dome, Orion hears Kage and Thanatos arguing like they know each other. Nerezza tells Orion the truth (Kage is working with villains and didn't call for help to protect his secret) and offers him a choice: his life or Kage's. Orion answers at "three": go after Kage. Nerezza sets a 45-second game: any limb her tentacles touch will be broken, and a limb touched twice will also be broken on Orion. Kage flies toward the full moon to weaken her shadows, falls, and is caught. Thanatos rules the last touch came after time. Then Nerezza breaks all four of Kage's limbs anyway, savagely. Thanatos lets Orion run. Orion runs. Thanatos rebuilds Kage's body with his own biomass, at enormous cost, and tells the unconscious boy that it will amplify his traits, "especially the negative ones."

**Details I caught.**

- Orion: "That's Shifting Horror. That's one of the most notorious villains currently on the underworld scene."
- The slap: "One fierce and quick slap that left a red handprint on Orion's face." Kage manipulates his friend to protect his secret, minutes after Abby died for him.
- "C'mon, Hermes! **Mush!!!**" He glues himself to Orion's back like a sled. It's a joke, and it's horrible, because it's a joke tonight.
- With a passenger, Orion can't use Zephyr Mode and tops out at "one hundred miles per hour." The speed figures are consistent: 200 normal max, 300 broken limit, 100 with a passenger.
- Thanatos and Nerezza: "What an elegant use of an enhancement." "It may have been attraction." "**You are not chaos like that boy, Nerezza. You are artistry.**"
- "The dark-skinned one is yours for the taking, however. I have no use for him." Thanatos casually gives Orion's life away.
- Nerezza's "contingency plan": a shadow wall twenty miles out. Her card says twenty miles is her limit, and she's used it as a fence. "It is causing a bit of a migraine." Her costs are real.
- **Thanatos on Kage:** "There's something about him. An almost endearing quality to his specific kind of helplessness. He seems…**moldable**. **A boy who's gone through what he has** and still smiling is someone very good at being fake. Either that, or he's just truly that 'inspired'… Damage, though—that can be repaired." Thanatos knows Kage's history (the orphanage? the store?). And "very good at being fake" is exactly what Kage becomes.
- "I haven't used my power as much as you. I'm pretty much still full." He's learned to track his lipids.
- Orion notices: "there was something different about the way Kage moved. Maybe it was confidence he was seeing?" He "had obviously seen Kage fight and use his enhancement on **three different occasions**." Spar, race, schoolyard. The count is right.
- The dome: "His head slammed against the top of the now dome-like structure." "**Kage twisted so he'd land under Orion and cushion his fall. If Orion's legs became damaged, he knew they couldn't get away.**" He takes the fall for Orion. Cracked ribs, fractured shoulder blade.
- "Sticky. Hey, Sticky. You okay? Sticky. Wake up!" Orion now says it without thinking.
- "I came bearing gifts, Thanatos. **It's** in near perfect condition." Kage is an "it."
- "It was gentle, but it wasn't comforting. **It was the way someone touches something that belongs to them.**"
- "Most predators tear their meal to shreds the moment they catch it. You? You deliver it untouched."
- "The darkness pulled away from his touch like it knew what he was."
- "**Monsters don't fear other monsters. They recognize each other.**" One of the best lines in the book.
- Kage fights back: "We can't just leave Hermes!… **I can't let someone else go because of my incompetence!**" He pounds the dome with concrete gauntlets. Whatever else he is, he's not abandoning Orion.
- Thanatos: "That boy is neither of our problems. Let him figure himself out."
