- **King Arthur's blindness** is a big new rule, and it raises questions. Can he turn it off? Is it his power or his psychology? Is it why his blades stop forming when he tries to hold back? A line or two in the aftermath, Lance admitting it to Cheyenne, would ground it.
- **Orion's reaction** is told through Kage's misreading. That's a smart choice, but readers might want one sentence from inside Orion before the section moves on, since we haven't been in his head since the Rebento fight.

**Score: 9/10.**

## Section 4 so far

Section 4 is named for Ailani, and it's about someone who knew Kage before he changed, trying to see whether he's still himself. It opens by showing us what the whole world was watching (Chapter NULL), then who Kage and Ailani were before any of this (Honey Toast). Then it puts Kage on trial in front of his class, exposes his successor secret, and finally gives Ailani a fight of her own. The new Chapter 7 is the section's best chapter, and it moves Ailani from reacting to acting. The next chapter will put her against Orion, the boy who likes her and is hiding the worst choice of his life, for the right to face Kage, the boy she loved and is hiding a villain's body.

**Section score so far: 8.2/10.**


# 5. Character Dossiers

Each dossier covers: what the book tells us, the power and its rules, the arc so far, the key lines, the relationships, what works, what I'd watch, and where I think the character is headed.

# The Four Leads

## Kage Ozul ("Sticky Situation," "King," "Prince," "Speed-Limiter," "Ozul," "red one," "ant")

**At a glance.** A second-year from Japan. Orphaned young, raised in foster homes, came to the American program for "a bed that didn't move every month." Arctic blue eyes, scarlet hair (lazy and face-covering until 2.10, then a self-cut, floating, "cartoonish" style with eyeliner). Lean, "not bulky." Seven inches shorter than Orion. Missing a tooth since 3.15. Worked at a maid café in first year (cat ears, maid outfit, "different-colored costumes," good tips). Knows Inazuma from middle school. Eats cheese for fuel.

**Power: Adhesive Manipulation.** He creates and controls "any and all forms of adhesives," excreted through his fingernails and toenails, fueled by lipids. No food, no power. Hardening requires cold drawn from his own body, so overuse causes fever (102°, 105°, then 110°F). Techniques we've seen: double-sided tape strips (the "flat peace sign"), glue sprays, toenail jets for propulsion and flight, hardened anchors, cement (from 1.6), concrete gauntlets, bouncy rubberized glue, key casts, glue "mines" compressed until they explode, confetti tape, a heat-resistant "fire-stop caulk" designed for Lucifer, and denser lipid-heavy tape. With certainty, he has telekinesis over his own adhesives (his hair stands on end). After 2.7, he regenerates through Thanatos's biomass, which costs energy and can't replace lost parts.

**The arc so far, in six stages.**

1. **The ghost** (Prologue). Lazy on purpose. "Staying small and safe." "Didn't unpack."
2. **The penitent** (1.1–1.11). After the store, he trains until his hands bleed, chasing a word ("brilliant") and a list of people to beat.
3. **The recruit** (1.12–1.15). Seduced by Thanatos because a villain told him his chaos was a gift. Spies on his idol. Wins his first fight against Orion with a feint built on real tears.
4. **The complicit** (2.1–2.7). Refuses to call for help, to protect his secret. Abby dies. He's broken and rebuilt by the villain he called.
5. **The returned** (2.10–3.13). New look, new body, new lies. Learns teamwork from Lance and Orion. Wins honestly against Elias.
6. **The amplified** (3.15–4.6). Without the whistle, he goes "feral" on live TV. Summons a demon in a crowded stadium and calls it honor. Lies naturally now. Cruel to Rebento. Afraid Anubis's love is conditional.

**What he wants, and what he needs.** He says he wants to be number one. What he wants underneath is to never again be "just the kid who froze," to be seen as someone who could have saved them. What he needs, I think, is to accept help. Every disaster in the book comes from his refusal: he won't blow the whistle, won't call Huoyan, won't let Zulfiya support him, won't tell Lance the truth. Every good thing comes from accepting it: Orion's "You improvise, I execute," Lance and Rebento throwing him over the crowd, Alex's cheer.

**Key lines.**

- "You don't hang posters if you're not sure how long the walls are yours." (narration)
- "A name that can turn something horrifying into something funny." (1.9)
- "Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life." (1.11)
- "People will call me King Kage one day. That's not arrogance, by the way. It's a spoiler." (1.11)
- "You can't break…what's already broken!" (2.12)
- "I am really bad at saving people. Everyone I've tried to save, it never ended well for them." (3.13)
- "I remembered to eat breakfast!" (3.11)
- "It's pretty clear to me that his love is conditional… I don't deserve that love." (4.6)

**His habits, as the book draws them.** He talks faster when excited ("Yes, yes, yes!", "s-s-s-suck"). He repeats words in triplets and octets ("I do, I do, I do!", "You said 'yet'!" ×8). He nicknames people he cares about (Hermes, Mercury, Speed-Limiter, Zebra, Partner, Sir Lance, Luci). He flirts as a reflex when hurt. He bows only to people he respects or owes. He grips things: the whistle, the backpack strap, Orion's leg in 1.2. And his hand curls around nothing whenever Frostbite comes up.

**Relationships.**

- **Orion:** rival, "not friend," the one person he understands in competition. Their bond is the book's spine.
- **Abby:** his "number one supporter," whose support he refused until she died for it. He carries her whistle and backpack.
- **Ailani:** his ex, the girl who knew him before. "Keep my number."
- **Lance:** his conscience, earned in one night.
- **Thanatos:** his maker, now in a literal sense. A mentor who treats him as a "project."
- **Frostbite:** a gruff, almost-father, who may be the reason the store exploded.
- **Anubis:** his idol, whose love "is conditional."
- **Huoyan:** the teacher who always saw his potential and keeps being proven right and ignored.
- **Lucifer:** his antagonist and mirror. Both carry a monster inside.

**What works.** He's funny and wounded at the same time, and the book never lets one cancel the other. His intelligence surprises (the epithets, the fire-stop caulk, the excretion loophole). His worst choices are motivated. And the book controls his decline: the cruelty was there before the biomass (the temple kick in 1.15), so the biomass amplifies something real instead of inventing it.

**What I'd watch.**

- **The gap between his intelligence and his blind spots.** The book leans on "illogically intelligent," which is great, but readers will want to see Kage reason about his own situation once. Does he ever wonder why Thanatos needed him? What "the guy in the chair" wants?
- **Accountability for the files.** He photographed his classmates' private files for villains. That should cost him something someday.
- **Abby.** He's carried her whistle and backpack for a whole section without ever saying her name to anyone except Frostbite. The moment he finally tells someone what happened will be one of the book's biggest scenes. Don't rush it, and don't skip it.

**Where I think he's going.** Toward the grand final, and then toward a reckoning: the villains will want payment, Orion's secret will surface, and the biomass will make him do something he can't take back. The book's title character must eventually decide what "Sticky Situation" means. I suspect the climax of his arc is him finally *asking* for help, out loud.

## Orion Vance ("Hermes," "Mercury," "Mr. Perception-And-Timing," "Big Vance," "Diaktoros," "Khrysorrhapis," "Angelos," "Polygios," "Kyllenios")

**At a glance.** Tall, dark-skinned, dreads, gold jewelry everywhere (rings, loc rings, lip piercings, earrings, necklaces, bracelets), amber-gold eyes. Seven inches taller than Kage and one inch taller than Lucifer. A model student. Loves slow walks, which are "a guilty pleasure he barely got to enjoy." Says "I am Greek, Kage, not Roman" about his mythological nickname. Has never lost anyone close to him, until he thinks he's lost Kage.

**Power: Celestial Velocity.** Gold-and-violet speed with "comet trails," up to 200 mph normally. Time "nearly stands still" for him. Costs: overheating, joint strain, and the danger that "a single misstep could tear him apart." Techniques: Aether Step (a burst dodge), Divine Relay (stored kinetic energy into a strike), Zephyr Mode (full power, which hurts to flare twice). Limits observed: 100 mph with a passenger; 300 mph when he broke his limit for Abby. Superhuman reflexes, which he insists are "careful training, timing, and control."

**The arc so far.** Orion starts as the kindest person in the book: he checks on Kage, befriends lonely Rhatella, takes half the blame for the race. His flaw is self-reliance ("I like doing things on my own"). Over the book, he's forced into partnership with Kage, learns Kage's darkest secret, and makes his own: in the dome, he chose Kage to suffer instead of himself, then ran when he was released. Since then he's carried two lies (Kage's villains, and his own choice), a crush on Ailani, and a growing hardness. Inazuma's racism cracks his calm. He low-blows Inazuma, drags Rebento's face on the ground, and turns "we're not friends" into a weapon. He's also the one who raises Abby's whistle for Kage when no one else cheers.

**Key lines.**

- "Speed isn't just running straight. It's perception and timing." (1.2)
- "You improvise. I execute. That's our combo." (1.6)
- "This isn't the Sticky Situation I've met! Wake up!" (2.6)
- "I'm gonna be the one to kill him." (2.2, as a joke; 2.8, as a confession)
- "We were not and never were friends… our competition, that was…for lack of a better and more accurate term, special." (2.13)
- "Oh, I have a hero name. I'm pro hero…Hermes." (3.6)
- "He improvised a plan for me to execute. Not bad." (4.5)

**Relationships.**

- **Kage:** his rival and "not friend," the person whose life he chose to trade. He hides that choice from everyone.
- **Ailani:** his crush (blushes in 2.6, 2.8, 2.11, 2.13, 3.12). He tells her Kage loved her anyway. He'll fight her next.
- **Rhatella:** his first friend in the book. He's barely seen her since Section 2. I'd love that thread to come back.
- **Abby:** the girl he didn't take time to know, and whose death he watched after saving her once.
- **Huoyan and Anubis:** Huoyan uses him as the reliable one; Anubis keeps getting him out of punishments, which Orion owes.
- **Inazuma:** the boy who made his race a weapon against him.

**What works.** He's a quiet character whose interior gets darker in a way only the reader sees. His dome choice is the most morally complex decision in the book, and the book treats it with care. His speed is used for emotion as much as for action (the stretched seconds before Ailani cries in 2.8).

**What I'd watch.**

- **Interiority.** Orion hasn't had a real interior chapter since 2.8. His fight with Ailani is the right moment for one.
- **His racism storyline.** The book raised it seriously. It deserves resolution: an apology from Inazuma, or Kage finally saying he was wrong to walk away, or Orion deciding for himself what he owes to people who won't defend him.
- **His hero name.** He adopted "Hermes" in a moment of defiance. Kage hates it ("I'm not yelling that in a crisis"). Is it his real name now? (We never learned the name he was about to say in 2.6.)

**Where I think he's going.** Into the fight against Ailani with everything unsaid. My guess is that the dome choice comes out at the worst possible time, probably through Nerezza, and that Orion's arc is about whether he can confess without being destroyed. If he forfeits to Ailani, it'll be for her. If he wins, he gets the rematch Kage wants, the one the ban forbids.

## Abby Neosame

**At a glance.** Small, pale, blonde, yellow eyes, a "twin-layered halo," wings she rarely showed (Kage first saw them as she died), nearly silent steps, a sweater, and a dolphin-shaped backpack full of water bottles. A support-hero student. Worked at a maid café. Religious: "God chose me to be the one who makes sure everybody's okay!" Had a brother who was "incredibly sick" and died when doctors wouldn't let her try to heal him. She carried his gold whistle.

**Power: Eyes of Divinity.** Healing (golden light, often with a stamina boost), wings, a halo, and the ability to "see through anyone": she scans injuries constantly and involuntarily ("My power scans on its own!"). Limits: the wounded must be alive, she can't heal herself, and serious wounds need speed. Her last technique, **Divine Judgement**, detonates her body in light, striking enemies "as well as herself." Her light hurts Nerezza.

**The arc.** Abby is the book's heart, and her arc is about waiting. As a child, she was told to wait while her brother died. As a first-year, she obeyed Lucifer's request to leave him alone, then watched him get worse for a year. She gave Kage the whistle and waited for a call that never came. Then she stopped waiting and walked the city all night looking for him, froze when she saw his wounds, and died keeping her promise.

**Key lines.**

- "I'm Abby, and your hands?" (1.1)
- "It's okay, Kage. Everything is going to be alright." (1.1, and her last words in 2.6)
- "Kage…I will be your number one supporter…" (1.5)
- "I'm happy to not be your friend, Lucifer." (2.1)
- "Is helping others not a desire?" (2.1)
- "I came here to support you." (2.6)

**Relationships.** Kage (devotion, maybe a crush; she kisses his cheek in 1.7). Lucifer (her patient; she kept his secret to the end). Orion (friend; she wanted the three of them to hang out). Zulfiya (the only other body-reader). Her brother.

**What works.** Everything about her is economical. She's in fewer scenes than almost any major character, and every one of them sets up her death. Her death uses every rule about her. And she has a wound of her own that mirrors Kage's exactly, so her death is also a choice made in her brother's memory.

**What I'd watch.** "Not every ability of Abby is known." "Some are worried she may actually have fallen from heaven." There's no body. She "burst completely into light." Kage blew her whistle (which only she can hear) for days. The book has quietly left a door open. My honest opinion: if she comes back, it must cost something enormous, or her death stops meaning what it means. If she doesn't, someone needs to say her name out loud soon. Only Huoyan is still looking for her.

## Ailani Shoma

**At a glance.** Latina, Black, and Japanese. Curly hair, often in a big bun. Tanned skin. Pink workout clothes. A purple, fluorescent dorm with string lights and a photo of Kage covered in glue she "kinda forgot" was there. Curses in Spanish when furious. Friends with Geneki, whom everyone else fears. Wants to be a hero to be "dependable" for her family, and to fund her real dream: being a top choreographer and dancer. Has "cried enough" for Kage.

**Power: Crystal Release.** A chromatic sigil appears on her hands, and she generates, shapes, and commands crystals from nothing: projectiles, walls, floors, butterflies, and weapons "as massive as a war hammer." Range: 25 meters. Large constructs last 10 to 15 seconds; small ones are nearly free. Telekinesis: the sigil rotates ninety degrees to float a construct. Cost: overuse crystallizes her own hands, from fingertips to wrists, until they lock and the sigil fades.

**The arc so far.** Ailani starts as a door that keeps slamming on Kage, and the book is careful to show it's justified: he flirted with "nine" women after their breakup, and with Reina after begging her back. She reopens the door in the mirror room, closes it when Lucifer tells her about Reina, grieves Kage when Orion says he's dead, slaps him when he returns, and tells him to keep her number. Through Section 3, she's mostly reactive: watching Kage for Orion, watching Orion for Kage, holding her tongue when Kage shakes his head. In Section 4, she finally insists on being heard ("This is my time, and I've been dying to perform"), and then she wins a fight with rhythm and friendship.

**Key lines.**

- "What's done is done, and what happened, happened." (1.3)
- "We aren't back together yet." (2.3)
- "And, Kage, lose my number." (2.4) / "And Kage? Keep my number." (2.14)
- "Maybe I'm used to crying for him?" (2.13)
- "He's worse." (3.15)
- "This is my time, and I've been dying to perform." (4.6)
- "Gen's my friend, after all, so I watched that replay way more times than she'd like." (4.7)

**Relationships.**

- **Kage:** first love, still. She quotes his promises back to him word for word.
- **Orion:** her training partner, her confidant, and (though she doesn't seem to know it) a boy who's in love with her. She chose to room with him to keep him safe.
- **Geneki:** her friend, the "big girl" nobody talks to.
- **Lance:** her first real opponent.

**What works.** She's sharp, funny, and fair. She's the one who names things: "He kind of reminds me of you," "He's worse," "If that's how you act around your 'not friends'…" Her section's new chapter finally lets her body tell her story: a dancer who counts beats, whose cost is her hands.

**What I'd watch.**

- **She needs more interiority.** We've seen her mostly through Kage and Orion. Section 4 is hers. Let us inside her head during the Orion fight.
- **Her dream needs texture.** "Top choreographer and dancer" is one line. A song, a routine, a memory would make it real.
- **Her hands are locked going into the next fight.** That's a great problem. Does she get healed (by Zulfiya?) or fight with crystal hands?

**Where I think she's going.** Into a fight with Orion that forces the triangle into the open, and possibly into a grand final against Kage, which would pay off "I don't want to fight you" (2.3) and "If we fought, I'd kick your ass, Kage!" She is also the person best positioned to discover Kage's secret. She's been looking for "an imperfection" since 3.1.


# The Class

## Lucifer Tulivalo ("Lu," "Luci," "Tulivalo," "demon boy," "the horned asshole")

**At a glance.** Tall (one inch shorter than Orion, which annoys him), orange hair, light blue eyes, dark red horns, and a tail that wags when he's happy and stiffens when he's tense. Came first in the entrance exam and trades the top spot with Elias. A solo dorm full of medals and trophies, with a black claymore engraved "Minkowski" on custom holders. Constantly tired. Calls people insects (ant, termite, bug, worm, pebble, mosquito, insect).

**Power: Hellspawn.** Fire from any part of his body at any temperature, from "a surgical burn" to steel-melting. And when he's "put fully to sleep," Abaddon emerges: ten feet tall, black-skinned, with burning horns and a tree-trunk tail, carrying all of Lucifer's fire plus enormous strength and skin too thick for concrete. Abaddon drains Lucifer's energy from inside constantly while trying to escape. Lucifer has trained himself to never fully sleep.

**The arc.** He's introduced as a bully. Section 2 reveals why: "I turn up my poor traits to get people to not want to talk to me… If I make more connections, then more people have a chance to see what's going on with me." He's only kind to Elias ("history") and Reina ("our enhancements work perfectly together"). Kage knocks him out, unknowingly freeing Abaddon for an hour. In the tournament, Lucifer makes a deal with Anubis to end Kage's rise, breaks Reina's support item and ignores her afterward, then releases Abaddon on live TV to win, mouthing "I'm sorry" to Elias first. He loses anyway. His secret is now public, and his best friend punched him for it.

**Key lines.** "You sticky fucking freak" (1.7). "I…turn up my poor traits." (2.1) "Angels can't befriend demons." (2.5) "What's in it for me?" (3.8) "Only Min gets to call me that." (3.16) "I exposed myself in front of millions of people and you couldn't just lose?!" (4.2)

**What works.** He's the book's best example of a character who's cruel for a reason without being excused for it. His tail is a perfect device. His mindscape (a demon in a child's chair at the family kitchen table) is the most striking image of his inner life.

**What I'd watch.** The book has told us Abaddon has killed ("even fewer have lived after seeing it"). Lucifer's backstory, and Elias's part in it, is the biggest unopened box in the cast. Also: Lucifer sent Abby to Kage ("BOTHER HIM!") on the night she died, and his tail went stiff asking about her. When he learns what happened, that should hit him hard.

**Where I think he's going.** Anubis said it: "You'll officially be Huoyan's problem." Huoyan, a fire user, already wants him. I think the book's quietest promising relationship is Huoyan and Lucifer.

## Abaddon

**At a glance.** The demon inside Lucifer. Speaks in the third person and in caps when free ("ABADDON IS IN THE FLESH ONCE MORE!"), but in the first person and at normal volume inside Lucifer's mind. Lives at the kitchen table of Lucifer's childhood home, "watching [Lucifer's life] through this domestic kitchen window." Calls Kage "red one," "cockroach," "child." Loves breaking bones and "letting people die on their own." Licks Kage's exposed bones.

**What he wants.** Freedom, and recognition: "We are one! You treat us as separate, but we couldn't exist without one another." His pitch to Lucifer is the book's darkest version of self-acceptance: "I'm everything you want to be! I'm strong. I'm powerful. I don't hesitate."

**The rules.** Emerges when Lucifer fully sleeps (2.12, KO), or apparently when Lucifer lets him (3.16). Returns when he's knocked unconscious (by ice in 2.12, by choking in 3.16). Feeds on Lucifer's energy; when he's been out recently, Lucifer is rested.

**What works.** He's frightening and funny, and he's smart: his read on Kage ("The truth is so terrible that you need to make all of this look like your grand plan?! OH, I LIKE YOU!") is one of the most accurate insights anyone has about Kage.

**What I'd watch.** The pronoun shift (third person outside, first person inside and after "we are one") is either a lovely deliberate detail or an inconsistency. I think it's deliberate. One line acknowledging it would lock it in.

## Elias Minkowski ("Min," "Minkowski," "that glasses wearer")

**At a glance.** Short (an inch taller than Reina), jet-black hair, glasses (Rhatella-made, broken by Kage, not replaced), a "boring outfit." Literal, precise, and deadpan ("Affirmative," "I will remain exclusively supportive"). Lucifer's best friend and co-top student. Sleeps over in Lucifer's room. Insists the claymore hangs perfectly straight. Reads lies. Anubis's file: "Not showing signs of trauma."

**Power: Chronal Inertia.** Anything he touches stops "dead in spacetime." Forces acting on it are banked and released all at once. Each hold has a three-minute clock that burns only when the world pushes back. Vital processes continue, which is why frozen people can talk, and why Kage could still excrete glue. It demands enormous concentration; his glasses track each hold's remaining time.

**The arc.** Elias looks like Lucifer's sidekick and turns out to be his warden. He wanted the whole trio in the Elite Program so he could keep watching Lucifer in case the demon got out, and "You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again." In the tournament, he humiliates Kage on purpose with a cruel, accurate speech, loses to Kage's excretion trick, and becomes the only person who deduces, out loud, that Kage is hiding something: "either I am unable to trust you because you're illogical, or I'm unable to trust you because you're hiding something. The worst part is, I believe the answer is both." When Abaddon emerges, he panics, runs, and later punches Lucifer without freezing him, "He wanted Lucifer to feel every hit," and hugs Kage.

**Key lines.** "Don't kill anyone while I sleep." (2.5) "If I said it's crooked, then it's crooked, Luci." (2.13) "You don't know my enhancement? That's unfortunate, but it explains your naive charge." (3.4) "Most people look at a car crash as they drive by… They drive off." (3.11) "We hate him and don't trust him, but we're not gonna kick him out with some stupid popularity contest." (4.3)

**What works.** He's the book's detective, and he's the best example of power-as-personality: a boy whose power is holding things still, holding a friend's monster still.

**What I'd watch.** Kage keeps saying Elias "knew something bad was going to happen" (2.11). Elias said, "I pray you guys come back okay," the night Abby died. He knows the bracket before it's set. His name is the mathematician who defined spacetime. Is Elias precognitive in some small way? Or just observant? Either answer is fine; the book should choose.

## Reina Cruz ("Cruz")

**At a glance.** Small, white hair, sparkling dark blue eyes, strong posture, thin "scrawny" arms. Lucifer's girlfriend; Elias's friend. Says "BOOM!" with her whole body. Commands Lucifer ("Put him down, and let's go!"), and he obeys. Fakes tears to trap him ("Oh, c'mon Min! I almost had him!").

**Power: Heat Charge.** Absorbs thermal energy into muscle mass. More heat, bigger and stronger and slower. Punching burns the energy and shrinks her; empty, she's dizzy and immobile. "The only one in the world that can truly say they can handle Lucifer's heat!" Her Rhatella-made backpack let her store and release energy to shrink and regrow at will, until Lucifer kicked it apart.

**The arc.** She's the one member of the trio who wasn't told about Abaddon. She finds out on live TV. She loses to her boyfriend, who breaks her gear and doesn't look at her afterward. Then she quietly moves to Kage's side of the room. She's also the first person to understand how Ailani beat Lance: "Woah, that's crazy smart!"

**What works.** She's sweeter and smarter than anyone gives her credit for, and her card pays off four setups at once.

**What I'd watch.** Reina deserves a scene with Lucifer about the backpack, the deal, and the secret. She's the person whose forgiveness would mean the most to him.

## Lance Faisceau ("Fai," "Lancey," "Sir Lance," "your kingship")

**At a glance.** Gray, reflective hair; a stern face. Class representative (or vice-rep, depending on whom you ask). Dates Cheyenne, who calls him "sugar" and "babe." Rich ("rich boy"). Dry humor ("You're putting me in slots, Kage"). From Quebec. His father was a high-earning villain who taught him to steal, do heists, and break and enter, until he was arrested and Lance saved some ants from a frog and realized he hated all of it.

**Power: Avalon's Legacy.** Yellow energy blades in any shape, from Rhatella-made hilts. They saw through rigid things like a cast saw but don't cut flesh (flesh moves with the blade, so a hit lands as a hot strike). That makes them safe against nearly anyone and lethal against armor (Volkan). He can form blades without holding the hilt. His limit is **King Arthur**: a berserker state that turns his eyes yellow, blinds him, sharpens his other senses, and makes him desperate to strike first. He stops forming blades to avoid it.

**The arc.** Lance arrives as a stern rule-follower who helps Kage beat Lucifer to the door ("Heroes help other heroes"). He challenges Kage to fight with honor, knights Volkan instead of beating him, tells Kage about his father in the hotel, defends Kage in the vote, and loses to Ailani after falling into King Arthur. He says sorry for getting "a little carried away."

**Key lines.** "Heroes help other heroes." (2.15) "Fight them how you'd want to be fought." (3.7) "To me, you are much more than a snail." (3.10) "Okay, Anubis." (3.13) "He's not the guy who saves the most people." (3.13) "I try to hold people to the standard I didn't hold to myself." (3.13) "When fighting sometimes you end up as someone else." (4.3)

**What works.** He's the book's moral center, and Chapter 7 made him more interesting by giving him a monster. Now his honor reads as discipline over something dangerous, not as naïveté.

**What I'd watch.** Lance knows breaking and entering. Kage has broken into three rooms. Lance will figure it out someday. And his father is alive and in prison somewhere in Canada. That's a story waiting to happen.

## Cheyenne Bandalore ("Partner," "Che")

**At a glance.** Unbuttoned blazer over a crisp white blouse, a loosened red necktie, a crimson-and-gold serape, spurred boots, and a cowboy hat "she never took off." Shadowed eyes. A strawberry lollipop she shifts "like a smoldering cigar." Calls everyone "partner," says "I reckon," "bless your heart," and "two shakes of a lamb's tail." Dating Lance, introduced by Rebento.

**Power: Unrevealed.** Her card was literally cut off mid-sentence when Elias froze her. She jingles "like keys or pocket change," she put her hands in her pockets before attacking, and she believes her power can't do telekinesis. Her surname is an old English word for a **yo-yo**. (See Theories.)

**The arc.** She meets Kage at his worst (he snaps at her in 1.10), sees him cry in 1.11, and decides to believe him. She recites his King speech word for word and gives him a lighter crown: "enjoy being a prince." She loses to Elias in seconds and bears it with grace.

**What works.** She's the book's warmest presence after Abby. Her costume paragraph is the best in the book.

**What I'd watch.** Her power has been teased for two sections. Pay it off when it matters, and make it worth the wait.

## Rebento Azucre ("Sugar Bomb")

**At a glance.** Brown hair. Says he's the main class rep. Chews gumballs, blows bubbles during lectures. Single; "his relationships don't last longer than a week." Crude ("Were her lips cold during kissing too?") and protective (he squares up to Orion for Cheyenne). Introduced Lance and Cheyenne.

**Power: Sweet-Toothed Surprise.** Turns the glucose in non-living objects into kinetic charge, so candy becomes explosives. Belt-mounted gumball machines (Rhatella-made). Can't charge living things (he tried, on Lance). Costs his own energy. Telekinesis tell: his eyes become rainbow swirls. Hero name: Sugar Bomb.

**The arc.** He helps throw Kage over the crowd, then becomes the only classmate to say what's obvious: Kage's healing is "not natural," and "one week and he's capable of being in the finals? That doesn't add up." He calls for a vote to remove Kage and loses it unanimously. Kage mocks him. He goes to sit with Lucifer and Elias. He fights a lot like Kage.

**What works.** He's right, and the book lets him be right while making him unpleasant. That's honest.

**What I'd watch.** "I'm never trusting Kage. If that even is his real name. You guys are gonna eat your fucking words." That's a promise. When Kage's secret comes out, Rebento should get his moment.

## Zulfiya Shifo

**At a glance.** Light blue hair that moves like fingers. Formal and gentle ("Please refrain from more motion"). Calls Huoyan "Xiansheng Huoyan." Calls Kage "King," "your majesty," "your highness," "my sovereign," and now "Prince." Rooms (silently) with Volkan.

**Power: Mending Mane.** Her hair heals by wrapping people slowly, restores stamina, and reads bodies to the exact degree and minute. The hair is normal-strength and slow to regrow if torn or burned.

**The arc.** She's the first to take Kage's King speech seriously, and the first to state the book's consent rule: "If he doesn't wish to be healed, he can't be forced." She forfeits her Elite Program chance to "support" Kage, triggers his worst outburst, reads the sorrow in his eyes instead of the anger, and takes it back for him. Afterward, her hair stays tucked behind her ear around him.

**What works.** She's kind in a strange, formal key. And she's the one person whose power could expose Kage's biomass with a single strand.

**What I'd watch.** Does she already know? Her hair never touches him after his return. If that's deliberate (she's respecting his boundary, or avoiding what she'd find), it's a great quiet thread.

## Severine Neant

**At a glance.** Five foot eleven, bluish-violet hair, purple eyes and clothes. Speaks formally, catalogs her "dislikes" (disruptions, incompetence, foolishness). Laughs until she cries. Drinks tea. Hadn't spoken to a classmate in a year and a half, except Kage.

**Power: Repulse-Redux.** Repulsive fields: barriers, flight, shockwaves. Costs stamina and concentration and weakens over time; recoil against powerful attacks. Her barrier is a half-dome she stands on, which is how she lost.

**The arc.** She beats Kage in Section 1 and laughs at his date request. She loses to Lucifer by ring-out, melts down on live TV, calls for Kage, and is dragged home by Kelly. Over tea, she decides Kage is now "significantly superior" and their rematch is inevitable.

**What works.** She's a great comic character with a sad edge, sharpened in Draft 3.

**What I'd watch.** Her rematch with Kage is now promised twice. And she's alone. Kage, of all people, is the only classmate she's ever spoken to.

## Rhatella Gardner (with C.O.R.E.)

**At a glance.** "Barely four-foot eight," brown face, a cleft lip, metallic wires growing from her head, swirling goggles, rat ears, a cyan bodysuit and lab coat. Hisses when startled. Gnaws on arms. Reads romance novels. Ships everyone. Calls herself "my ugly ass." Stamps her face on everything she builds. Builds gear in alphabetical order. Has a crush on Kage ("Lord knows he wouldn't take my heart!"). Daughter of Gene; sister of Geneki.

**Power: Wireplay.** Grows and controls cables, takes over machines, carries electricity harmlessly, senses disruptions in connected devices. Might "one day manipulate solid metal itself."

**C.O.R.E.** Her AI: the Centralized Operational Response Engine. Three objectives (respond to stimuli; protect Rhatella; protect itself). Cyan eyes on black monitors. Draws blood samples. Sighs audibly. Kicks Kage down a shaft with a giant boot.

**Her quiet influence.** She built Elias's glasses, Rebento's gumball machines, Reina's energy pack, and Lance's hilts (two of them). Half of the tournament's key moments run on her gear. Two of her creations are destroyed on worldwide TV.

**What I'd watch.** She has a sample of Kage's blood from before the biomass. She missed the healing on TV by seconds. She's the person in the book most able to figure out what's inside him, and the person most likely to be hurt by it. She's been offstage since 1.9 (except 4.0). Bring her back.

## Inazuma Sokudo

**At a glance.** Blue hair, dark blue eyes. "WAZZZUPPPPPPPP!!!" "It's the red to my blue!" Has known Kage since middle school in Japan. Texted Kage all through the ghost year; sent the "Remember this?" maid photo. Believes his surname (Japanese for "speed") makes him destined to be the fastest.

**Power: Lightning Fast.** Blue speed; friction static stored and discharged as lightning; must discharge or burn out.

**The arc.** He calls Orion a "big black dolt," refuses to apologize ("don't be a snowflake"), repeats it in the ring, adds a deniable "when you end up a criminal" jab, and loses to a low blow. Kage defends him because he was nice to Kage "when nobody else was."

**What I'd watch.** He's the "another speedster" from 1.2, and he's the clearest dark mirror of Kage (Ailani: "he's like you if you had Orion's enhancement"). The book raised his racism seriously. Don't leave it hanging.

## Volkan Salyangozu ("Knight Volkan")

**At a glance.** Big (about 500 pounds), dark red eyes, always in a black hoodie. Late to class in 1.7 and punished with fifty laps. Forgotten by Kage for two sections ("Have I spoke to Volkan before?").

**Power: Volcano Snail.** Built like the real scaly-foot snail: an iron shell and iron-scaled body, heat resistance from hydrothermal vents, gills. Tucks and rolls. Slow, but "tries to think fast."

**The arc.** "He just wanted to last long enough for them to remember his name." Lance knights him, the stadium chants his name, and he mouths "thank you." Later, he shows up to wish Kage luck, which nearly makes Kage cry.

**What I'd watch.** He tolerates extreme heat. That makes him one of the few students who could stand next to Lucifer (or Abaddon) without burning. A future team-up waiting to happen.

## Geneki Gardner ("Gen")

**At a glance.** About eight feet tall, enormously muscular, one lazy eye, rat ears like her sister's, a tail cut to a stump. Rarely shows emotion. "Doesn't really talk to many people." Ailani's friend. Gene's daughter.

**Power:** Unnamed. Green energy around her leg, strong enough to disintegrate the arena floor.

**The arc.** Two lines: she loses by breaking through the floor, and she asks her mother to let her go home, "Alone, preferably." Then her loss becomes the rule Ailani uses to win.

**What I'd watch.** The stump tail. Her mother's public correction. A girl everyone calls a monster, whom one friend calls kind. There's a whole story here.

## Youseff Nasser

**At a glance.** "A small brown-skinned boy," about five foot three. Anubis's little brother. Picks his nose in front of a stadium chanting his name. Fakes a trip out of bounds the instant his match starts. "I hope the Elite Program is fun for you. Because it sounds like torture to me."

**Power:** Unrevealed. Anubis's first choice for successor, abandoned because "his heart's just not in it."

**What I'd watch.** Elias, who studied everyone, didn't study Youseff. Huoyan smirked at his fake trip. He's refusing a legacy, not failing at one.

## Alexander Lund ("Alex")

**At a glance.** Green hair, flower earrings, anxious. His mother cheers "I'm so proud of you for trying!" from the front row, which costs him his match. Rooms with Elias in silence.

**Power:** Unrevealed. (See Theories for a guess based on his name and look.)

**The arc.** He's the scared kid Kage sticks his tongue out at in 3.16, and the first person in the stadium to cheer Kage on. His mother "had never seen her son care so deeply about any friend before."

## Kelly Iasacht's son

Unnamed and unseen. A student in the program who "didn't end up showing up" to the tournament. Possibly the nineteenth classmate. Kelly: "I can never win, I swear."

# The Adults

## Huoyan Niao ("Hellfeather," "Mr. Niao," "Xiansheng Huoyan," "Hon")

**At a glance.** Tall, pale, bright white hair, wings he usually hides. A strict homeroom teacher who "loathe[s] all of my students equally." Slicks his hair back when angry. English is his second language (Anubis corrects his grammar). Sleeps at the school: "I sleep here." Used to be Anubis's sidekick. "Barely drink[s] anymore." Runs an agency and wants Lucifer in it. Has an optional Thursday class no one attends.

**Power: Phoenix.** A body alteration (wings) and a controlled enhancement (fire): "two in one." Flaming feathers he can fire or block with. Weakness: water on his wings puts out the flames, and they take a while to reignite.

**The arc.** He starts as the strict teacher everyone fears and is revealed, layer by layer, as the adult who actually cares. He secretly hoped Kage and Orion could rise together. He's the only adult who keeps searching for Abby, refreshing his phone through the whole tournament. He leaves his handprints scorched into Anubis's desk. He defends even Lucifer: "I may not agree with Lucifer, but I defend my students." "Pacifists make great rescue heroes."

**Key lines.** "I have morons for students." "I loathe all of my students equally, and I haven't told Anubis everything." "You're supposed to know where everyone is all the time, Mr. Know-it-all!" "I sleep here." "I teach all heroes, not just ones who punch well."

**What I'd watch.** "I haven't told Anubis everything." It's been unanswered since 1.11. And his past with Anubis is the book's most intriguing adult story. Also: he punishes students physically (head slams, a fireball), which the book plays for comedy. It's worth deciding how the book wants readers to feel about that.

## Khaled Nasser ("Anubis," "Number One," "Mr. Know-it-all," "Khaled")

**At a glance.** Tall, gray hair, sunglasses he lowers "an inch" when he means business. The world's number one hero and head of the hero program. A bare office with one Anubis action figure ("I don't decorate my office because that would be a waste of money"). Says "Sigh" out loud. Calls Huoyan "Hon." Has a little brother (Youseff). Wants cameras in student dorms. Needs a successor "someday soon."

**Power: Unrevealed.** "His own enhancement seemed to activate" (1.2). He catches lies instantly ("Kage, that's a lie"). He has drones and photographs from impossible angles. He watches. (See Theories.)

**The arc.** Anubis is charming, generous, sincere, and mercenary, all at once. He spares Kage and Orion from punishment, sees something "real" in Kage, and means it. He also files photos of children, attends a sponsor dinner while students are dying, says "That's not important right now!" about a missing girl, bets Kage's support on a deal with Lucifer for ratings, and stops Huoyan from saving a student who's "going to die down there." He knew Kage was alive on Thursday and told no one.

**Key lines.** "You two were brilliant." "My agency will be watching." "You don't put on a heroic act; you just act heroic." "An orphaned failure turned number one hero just sells itself!" "You weren't Sticky Situation in your first year." "Win by any means necessary." "A hero who has secrets in his pocket is the best kind!" "If he wanted to quit, he would have."

**What I'd watch.** The book is careful not to make him a villain, and that's right. The danger is that he becomes a caricature of capitalism. His best scenes are the ones where he's half-right. Keep him half-right. And the question Huoyan asks, "I get seeing yourself in someone," suggests Anubis has a past that looks like Kage's. That would be a great reveal.

## Gene Gardner

**At a glance.** Tall, rat-like (ears, snout, whiskers), black hair, a green suit and green glasses. Creator of the Elite Tournament and Elite Program. The tournament's announcer ("Oh, I just adore announcing and hosting stuff like this"). Mother of Rhatella and Geneki. Makes up rules on the spot ("as stated in the rules, the ones I just made up").

**The arc.** She's showy and loves the crowd, and she corrects her daughter in front of millions. But she hated Anubis's deal ("I'm not going to gamble away a kid's future"), and her whiskers twitched when she heard it. She switches the match order twice to wreck his plan. "Sticking it to Anubis is fun…I need to do it more often with you guys."

**What I'd watch.** Her real motives. Is she protecting kids, protecting her tournament's integrity, or just enjoying Anubis's discomfort? And what is the Elite Program, really? She promised to "go deeper on the details when we decide who joins."

## Kelly Iasacht

**At a glance.** Blond, blue-eyed, Irish ("It's an Irish remedy"). A consistent top hero who wants Anubis's spot, with less government funding because he won't teach. A wife who yells at him to call, and a son who didn't show up. Gets stuck with every unpleasant job.

**Power:** Unrevealed. "I had to use my enhancement on her." His surname is the Irish word for a loan or borrowing. (See Theories.)

**What works.** He's the outsider adult who can say what Huoyan can't: "now the only people left are the pawns that put on a good show for you, huh, Anubis?" And he's kind to Huoyan, taking his phone and telling him to rest.

# The Villains

## Thanatos Augustus ("Shifting Horror," "Horror," "Thany")

**At a glance.** Six feet tall, a large scar from his face down his neck that he rubs with his thumb, purple eyes, black hair fading to white at the tips, a black suit and shirt with a white tie. Speaks calmly and precisely. Motto: "Figure out what makes them tick, and try to give them what they desire." Was in Russia on a mission for "the boss." Flirts with Nerezza. Furious at Frostbite for letting Kage compete.

**Power: Bio-Forge.** Cellular control of his own body (density, shape, appendages), absorbing anything he touches, biological or not. Regenerates. Costs: cellular overdrive, rejection by some materials, migraines and blackouts if he loses focus. Hunger: "He is never finished consuming." He gave Kage pieces of his own biomass, leaving himself smaller, starving, and shaking.

**What he wants.** "I know he can do what I need him to." We don't know what that is. He calls Kage "my project" and "moldable." He knows "what he has" gone through.

**What works.** He's a seductive villain whose pitch is half true, and the book shows him sacrificing for Kage in a way no hero does. That complexity is the book's best villain writing.

**What I'd watch.** The biomass inside Kage contains "bits of the things he'd absorbed over the years," with "their instincts trying to claw their way out." That's a time bomb, and he knows it.

## Frostbite ("Frost")

**At a glance.** A glowing blue-and-black suit and mask with compartments (clothes, makeup, contact lenses). Underneath: pale skin, fingertips "dark and blistered, like the cold was eating him from the inside out." Calls Kage "kid." Sighs constantly. Bets on Kage in a bar, where his scotch ice never melts. Says he's "not really good with kids."

**Power: Polar Shift.** Total creation and control of ice. Cost: frostbite from the inside out. Weak to fire and explosions.

**The arc.** He calls Kage a pawn and tries to kill him. Then he softens: apologizes, pats his head, brings him clothes and makeup, accuses Thanatos of violating Kage's body "without consent," gives him a "test" to bring back Lucifer, lingers over the unconscious Lucifer and almost touches his face, lets Kage enter the tournament, and tells a stranger Kage is "a student of mine." He has an "other half" to his reasons he won't say aloud.

**What I'd watch.** Everything. Kage's hand curls around nothing every time Frostbite comes up. Frostbite uses Lucifer's exact insult. He lingers over Lucifer. He's the book's best mystery. (See Theories: he may have been at the convenience store, and he may be connected to Lucifer.)

## Nerezza

**At a glance.** A woman in black with a mask, vain about her face ("How dare you ruin my beautiful face with your disgusting fluid?!"). Lives in a crappy apartment in Russia. Watches only the news, and children screaming. Prides herself on "killing with grace." Flirts with Thanatos and makes him sleep on the couch.

**Power: Umbrakinesis.** Shadows as parts of her body: tentacles (sharp, toothed suckers), walls, domes, travel through shadow. Sees in the dark, moves silently. Limits: only within twenty miles of somewhere she's walked; overuse blinds her, locks her hands, and causes pain; light weakens her shadows (Abby's light, the full moon).

**What she did.** Murdered an official pushing the Security Accords, destroyed Anubis's drone, killed Abby from her own shadow, offered Orion the choice in the dome, broke all four of Kage's limbs, and enjoyed every second.

**What I'd watch.** She knows Orion's secret. She wants a "serious talk" with Thanatos. She can't kill Kage yet ("The only reason you're still alive is because-!"). She's the person most likely to make the book's next disaster happen.

## "The guy in the chair" (the boss)

Mentioned once by Frostbite (1.13), who almost says "I don't care what he…" before switching to "the guy in the chair." He sends Thanatos to Russia and Nerezza after politicians. He wants the Security Accords stopped. He may have a personal interest in Kage. A wheelchair? A throne? A director's chair? A desk? It's a strange, specific image, and that's good.

# Minor and Unseen Characters

- **The soul up above.** The person who died under the awning. No name, no age, no gender. Kage holds their wrist and then holds air. "I need that soul up above looking down and knowing, really knowing, that I became a real hero." Giving them a name, someday, would be a gut-punch.
- **Abby's brother.** Sick, untreated, dead. Made the gold whistle "only I can hear." Abby doesn't know how it works.
- **Lance's father.** A high-earning villain in Quebec, arrested. Taught his son crime as their only bonding time.
- **Alex's mother.** The only unconditionally proud parent in the book: "I'm so proud of you for trying!"
- **Kelly's wife.** Yells at Kelly to call Anubis.
- **The murdered official.** A suit outside a government building. Pushing the Security Accords.
- **The drunk bettor.** Lost $300 on "demon boy."
- **The crowd.** A character in its own right: it cheers, boos, chants "Knight Volkan," demands blood, and finally chants Kage's name because a scared boy did first.
- **The maid-café coworkers.** "Those guys there must've really liked him." And the customers who gave "fat tips."
- **The general-studies girls** Kage dated in first year.
- **The unnamed twentieth classmate,** if the class is still twenty.


# 6. Names, Decoded

A lot of names in this book mean something in the language they come from, and several of them are jokes that most readers will never notice. I've marked how confident I am in each reading. Where I'm guessing, I say so.

| Name | Language | Meaning | Why it fits |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Kage** | Japanese (影) | "shadow" | The boy who stayed invisible on purpose, a "ghost," who lives in his rival's shadow and now hides a villain's body. Also: in *Naruto*, "Kage" is the title of a village's leader (Hokage, Kazekage). "King Kage" is a double title. *Confident on the meaning; the Naruto echo is a fun bonus.* |
| **Ozul** | uncertain | — | I can't find a clean meaning. It sounds like "ooze," which suits a boy who excretes glue. *A guess.* |
| **Orion** | Greek myth | the great hunter, a constellation | A celestial name for a "Celestial Velocity" user who leaves comet trails. *Confident.* |
| **Hermes** | Greek myth | messenger god, fastest of the gods, guide of souls | Kage's nickname, and now Orion's hero name. Every epithet Kage uses in 1.15 is authentic: *Diaktoros* (guide, messenger), *Khrysorrhapis* ("of the golden wand"), *Angelos* (messenger), *Polygios* (a cult title from Troezen), *Kyllenios* ("of Mount Kyllene," his birthplace). *Confident.* |
| **Abby** | Hebrew (Abigail) | "my father's joy" | A daughter-and-sister name for a girl whose life was shaped by her brother's death. *Confident on the root.* |
| **Neosame** | uncertain | — | Possibly echoes Japanese *nezame* (寝覚め, "awakening"), which would suit an angel. *A guess.* |
| **Ailani** | Hawaiian | "chief," "high chief" | She calls Kage "chief" in 4.1. If that's a wink, it's a great one. *Confident on the meaning.* |
| **Kailani** | Hawaiian | "sea and sky" | Kage's ship name for himself and Ailani is a real, lovely name. *Confident.* |
| **Shoma** | Japanese | many readings (e.g., 翔馬, "soaring horse") | Fits her Japanese heritage. *Uncertain which reading.* |
| **Rhatella** | invented | "rat" + "-ella" (a diminutive) | "Little rat." Rat ears, hissing, gnawing. *Confident.* |
| **Gardner** | English | "gardener" | Gene grows things: a tournament, a program, two daughters. *Speculative.* |
| **Gene** | English | short for Eugene/Eugenia; also "gene" | The woman who designs a program to select the best students. "Gene" as in genetics feels pointed. *Speculative.* |
| **Geneki** | uncertain | possibly echoes Japanese *genki* ("energetic, healthy") | If so, it's ironic for a girl described as lacking "personality." *A guess.* |
| **Lucifer** | Latin | "light-bringer," the morning star; the fallen angel | *Confident.* |
| **Tulivalo** | Finnish | *tuli* ("fire") + *valo* ("light") | His surname is "firelight," which is close to a translation of "Lucifer." His name means light-bringer twice. *Confident on the parts.* |
| **Abaddon** | Hebrew | "destruction," "place of destruction"; in Revelation, the angel of the bottomless pit | Kage's cover story in 4.2 uses exactly this meaning: "I wanted destruction, or as the bible would put it, I wanted Abaddon." *Confident.* |
| **Elias** | Hebrew (Elijah) | "my God is Yahweh"; the prophet taken to heaven alive | A watchful, prophetic boy who seems to know what's coming. *Speculative on intent.* |
| **Minkowski** | surname | Hermann Minkowski, who unified space and time into "spacetime" | Elias's power stops things "dead in spacetime." It's the best name-power match in the book. *Confident on the reference.* |
| **Reina Cruz** | Spanish | "queen" + "cross" | A queen in a book about kings and princes. *Confident.* |
| **Lance** | English / Arthurian | a lance (spear); Lancelot | The knight who knights Volkan, with a power called "Avalon's Legacy" and a berserker state called "King Arthur." *Confident.* |
| **Faisceau** | French | "beam" (as in a beam of light), also "bundle" | His blades are beams of yellow light. And he's from Quebec. *Confident.* |
| **Cheyenne** | English (from the Cheyenne nation; also a Wyoming city) | — | The cowgirl's name points at the American West. *Confident on the reference.* |
| **Bandalore** | old English | an 18th–19th-century word for a **yo-yo** | A clue to her unrevealed power? (See Theories.) *Confident on the meaning.* |
| **Rebento** | Portuguese | "I burst / I explode" (from *rebentar*); also "sprout" | *Confident on the root.* |
| **Azucre** | Galician | "sugar" | Rebento Azucre is "I explode, sugar." His hero name is Sugar Bomb. His name was his hero name all along. *Confident.* |
| **Zulfiya** | Persian/Uzbek | from *zulf*, "curl, lock of hair" | A girl whose power is her hair. *Confident on the root.* |
| **Shifo** | Uzbek (from Arabic *shifa*) | "healing, cure" | Zulfiya Shifo is close to "Healing Tresses." Her power is called Mending Mane. *Confident.* |
| **Severine** | French (from Latin *severus*) | "severe, stern" | *Confident.* |
| **Neant** | French (*néant*) | "nothingness, void" | A girl who pushes everything away and sits alone in an empty field. "Severe nothingness." *Confident.* |
| **Inazuma** | Japanese (稲妻) | "lightning" | His power discharges lightning. *Confident.* |
| **Sokudo** | Japanese (速度) | "speed" | He brags about it himself: "My last name is Sokudo, after all!" *Confident.* |
| **Volkan** | Turkish | "volcano" | *Confident.* |
| **Salyangozu** | Turkish | "snail" (*salyangoz*, in an inflected form) | Volkan Salyangozu is "Volcano Snail," which is also the name of his power. The 1.7 lateness gag was hiding in his name. *Confident.* |
| **Youseff** | Arabic (Yusuf / Joseph) | "he will add" | Joseph was the favored younger brother. Youseff was Anubis's first pick for successor, and wants none of it. *Speculative on intent.* |
| **Nasser** | Arabic | "victorious," also "helper, supporter" | A family named "supporter," in a book obsessed with what support costs. *Confident on the meaning; the thematic link is mine.* |
| **Khaled** | Arabic | "eternal, immortal" | The man who needs a successor "someday soon." *Confident on the meaning.* |
| **Anubis** | Egyptian myth | god of the dead, embalming, and the **weighing of the heart** | He weighs students in files and catches lies instantly. Fittingly, the ancient Greeks and Romans in Egypt merged Anubis with Hermes into a single god, **Hermanubis**. In this book, "Anubis" chooses Kage, and "Hermes" is his rival. *Confident on the mythology; whether you intended Hermanubis, I can't say, but it's a delightful accident if not.* |
| **Huoyan Niao** | Mandarin (火焰鸟) | "flame bird" | A phoenix. His power is called Phoenix. *Confident.* |
| **Hellfeather** | English | — | Fire plus wings. *Obvious, and good.* |
| **Kelly** | Irish | from Ó Ceallaigh; often glossed "bright-headed" or "strife" | *Confident on origin.* |
| **Iasacht** | Irish | "a loan, borrowing" (*ar iasacht*, "on loan") | Possibly a clue to his unrevealed power. (See Theories.) *Confident on the meaning.* |
| **Thanatos** | Greek | the personification of death | A second death god "mentoring" Kage alongside Anubis. *Confident.* |
| **Augustus** | Latin | "venerable, majestic"; the first Roman emperor's title | New in Draft 3. "Death the Emperor," recruiting the boy who wants to be King. *Confident.* |
| **Nerezza** | Italian | "blackness" | The shadow-woman. *Confident.* |
| **Frostbite** | English | — | His power literally gives him frostbite. |
| **Alexander Lund** | Greek + Scandinavian | Alexander: "defender of people"; Lund: "grove" | Green hair, flower earrings, and a surname meaning "grove." (See Theories.) *Confident on meanings; the power link is my guess.* |
| **C.O.R.E.** | acronym | Centralized Operational Response Engine | The heart (core) of Rhatella's lab. |

The pattern is striking. **The book names its cast like a mythology.** Death gods mentor the protagonist. A fallen angel and his abyss share a body. A queen dates the devil. A knight, a phoenix, a snail-volcano, a girl named "nothingness" who pushes everything away, a boy named "spacetime" who stops time. If you keep doing this, a reader who looks names up will be rewarded on every page.

# 7. The Powers Compendium

## How powers work in this world

The book gives us a small but consistent rulebook, mostly in 1.10:

- **Everyone has an enhancement.** "Every human on earth was born with an enhancement in their blood."
- **Two broad categories.** "Body alterations" (Huoyan's wings, Volkan's shell, Lucifer's horns and tail, Rhatella's ears and wires) and controlled enhancements (fire, glue, crystals). Some people, like Huoyan, have both: "It's like I have two in one."
- **Telekinesis.** "Most people with enhancements that aren't mutations… are capable of" basic telekinesis over what they produce. It requires focus on "a singular object" first, then shape control. For Kage, it requires **certainty**: "Back then, he'd been sure he was better than whoever stood in front of him. Somewhere around the mirror room, he'd stopped being sure" (2.12).
- **Telekinesis has visible "tells."** Kage's hair stands on end. Ailani's sigil rotates ninety degrees. Rebento's eyes become rainbow swirls. Lance points out tells as a combat skill.
- **"Enhancements shouldn't feed on their users"** (Abby, 2.1). Abaddon does.
- **Support items.** Rhatella builds gear, in alphabetical order by surname, for anyone who needs it.

## Every enhancement in the book

| Character | Enhancement | What it does | Costs and limits | Tell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kage | Adhesive Manipulation | Creates and controls any adhesive (tape, glue, cement, concrete, bouncy glue, fire-stop caulk) from his nails | Burns lipids; hardening draws cold from his body, causing fever; overuse brings exhaustion | Hair stands on end (telekinesis) |
| Kage (since 2.7) | Thanatos's biomass | Rapid regeneration (black tendrils, black blood) | Costs energy and causes exhaustion; can't regrow detached parts; amplifies negative traits; flares with injury and high heart rate | "Feral" eyes; black blood |
| Orion | Celestial Velocity | Speed up to 200 mph (300 at his limit), reflexes, wall-running; Aether Step, Divine Relay, Zephyr Mode | Overheating, joint strain, risk of a "misstep"; 100 mph with a passenger | Gold-violet shimmer |
| Abby | Eyes of Divinity | Healing, constant involuntary body-scanning, wings, halo; Divine Judgement (self-detonation in light) | Can't heal herself; target must be alive; must act fast | Glowing halo; eyes glow deep yellow before Divine Judgement |
| Ailani | Crystal Release | Crystal projectiles, walls, floors, weapons, figures | 25-meter range; big constructs 10–15 seconds; overuse crystallizes and locks her hands | Chromatic sigil; sigil rotates for telekinesis |
| Rhatella | Wireplay | Grows and controls wires; takes over machines; carries electricity | Unknown | — |
| Lucifer | Hellspawn | Fire from any body part, any temperature; Abaddon when fully asleep | Abaddon drains him constantly; he can't truly sleep | Tail (wags/stiffens) |
| Abaddon | (part of Hellspawn) | Ten-foot demon, all of Lucifer's fire plus huge strength and armor-thick skin | Can be knocked out; needs air; hurts himself with violent launches | Burning horn tips; third-person speech |
| Elias | Chronal Inertia | Touch freezes anything in spacetime; banks all forces and releases them at once | Three-minute clock per hold, burning only when the world pushes back; heavy concentration; vital processes continue | Adjusts his glasses |
| Reina | Heat Charge | Absorbs heat into muscle mass | Bigger means slower; punching shrinks her; empty means dizzy and immobile | Size |
| Severine | Repulse-Redux | Repulsive barriers, flight, shockwaves, blasts | Stamina, concentration, recoil; weakens over time | Purple barrier |
| Zulfiya | Mending Mane | Healing and body-reading through her hair | Hair is normal-strength; slow to regrow | Moving hair |
| Cheyenne | Unrevealed | — | She believes it can't do telekinesis | A jingle "like keys or pocket change"? |
| Lance | Avalon's Legacy | Yellow beam blades of any shape that saw through rigid things like a cast saw but don't cut flesh; can form blades without holding the hilt | Heat (solved by Rhatella's hilt); "King Arthur" berserker state that blinds him | Eyes glow yellow |
| Rebento | Sweet-Toothed Surprise | Turns glucose in non-living things into explosive kinetic charge | Can't charge living things; costs his own energy | Eyes go rainbow (telekinesis) |
| Inazuma | Lightning Fast | Speed; stores friction static and discharges lightning | Must discharge or burn out; misstep risk | Blue blaze |
| Volkan | Volcano Snail | Iron shell and scales, tuck-and-roll, heat resistance, gills | Slow | Shell |
| Geneki | Unnamed | Green-energy strength that can disintegrate concrete | Unknown | Green energy at her leg |
| Youseff | Unrevealed | — | — | — |
| Alexander | Unrevealed | — | — | — |
| Huoyan | Phoenix | Fire; flaming feathers to fire or block; wings | Water on his wings kills the flames for a while | Spread wings |
| Anubis | Unrevealed | Something activates when he leaves in 1.2; catches lies; watches | — | Lowers sunglasses "an inch" |
| Gene | Unknown | — | — | — |
| Kelly | Unrevealed | "I had to use my enhancement on her" | — | — |
| Thanatos | Bio-Forge | Cellular reshaping; absorbs anything he touches; regenerates; can transfer his biomass | Cellular overdrive; rejection; migraines and blackouts; constant hunger | Absorbed material visibly merging into him |
| Frostbite | Polar Shift | Total ice creation and control | Frostbite from the inside out; weak to fire and explosions | Glowing suit; ice that won't melt |
| Nerezza | Umbrakinesis | Shadow tentacles, walls, domes; travel through shadows; sees in the dark | 20-mile radius from where she's walked; overuse blinds her and locks her hands; light weakens her | Visible smile through her mask |

## Rhatella's support items, in alphabetical order

| Owner | Item | Fate |
|---|---|---|
| **A**zucre (Rebento) | Belt-mounted gumball machines | Intact |
| **C**ruz (Reina) | Back-mounted energy pack (store and release heat) | Destroyed by Lucifer (3.14) |
| **F**aisceau (Lance) | Two heat-proof hilts | Intact |
| **M**inkowski (Elias) | Glasses that track hold timers | Destroyed by Kage (3.11) |
| **O**zul (Kage) | Unfinished ("a human insulator is the WORST!") | Abandoned by Kage ("only the old Kage was after") |

Every student after "O" (Shifo, Shoma, Sokudo, Salyangozu, Tulivalo, Vance) has no Rhatella gear. Orion turned his down. That's the alphabet holding perfectly across 3,000 lines.

## Does the book keep its own rules?

Almost always. That's one of its greatest strengths. Here's a checklist.

**Kept:**

- Kage's fever climbs with hardening (102°, 105°, 110°) and spikes in 3.16 while he refills concrete gauntlets.
- Kage goes barefoot whenever he needs toenail glue (1.8, 2.6, 2.7).
- Kage runs out of lipids in long flights (1.6, 2.7) and eats cheese and minifridges to compensate.
- Kage's telekinesis works when he's sure (1.15) and fails when he isn't (2.3, 2.12).
- Kage's regeneration costs energy (3.16) and can't regrow a tooth (4.4).
- Huoyan's fireball burns Kage before the biomass (2.2); Lucifer's fire seems not to after (3.15). Huoyan notices.
- Orion's speeds: 200 normal, 300 broken limit, 100 with a passenger.
- Abby can't heal herself, which is why she dies.
- Abby's light hurts Nerezza, and Kage uses moonlight against her.
- Nerezza's twenty-mile limit becomes her trap.
- Elias's frozen opponents can talk; Kage can excrete.
- Elias's clock barely burns when Kage doesn't struggle.
- Lance's blade spares flesh and cuts rigid things, so it could kill Volkan.
- Lance's blades saw rather than slice (3.10's "slowly sawed," 4.7's "cast saw").
- Ailani's sigil appears every time she uses her power, rotates for telekinesis, and her hands lock on schedule.
- Abaddon emerges when Lucifer is knocked out and returns when Abaddon is.
- The arena's out-of-bounds rule (touching under the floor) is used twice, in 3.3 and 4.7.

**Soft spots:**

- **Abaddon's trigger.** "When Lucifer is put fully to sleep" (card), but in 3.16 he seems to release Abaddon voluntarily by burning his own head. Anubis says, "I didn't think he could control it." Clarify.
- **Avalon's Legacy's card** says it cuts rigid things "like it isn't even there," but 3.10 and 4.7 show it sawing slowly. The cast-saw version is better; consider adjusting the card.
- **Telekinesis and "mutations."** Huoyan says body-alteration powers can't do telekinesis. Kage's power is excretion, "super similar to sweating," which sounds body-based, yet he has telekinesis. That's either a clue (see Theories) or a gap.
- **Kage's 110°F fever.** Medically, that's fatal or brain-damaging. Zulfiya says so. Kage shrugs: "maybe my DNA is meant for that." It's either a clue or a stretch.


# 8. Themes

## 1. Being seen versus saving people

The book's central question. The Prologue sets it up ("Agencies chase ratings more than rescues"), and every section tests it.

- **The watchers:** Anubis (files, drones, cameras, merch, "THAT IS HOW YOU PUT ON A SHOW!"), Gene ("It's not what I think. It's what they think."), Nerezza ("Children screaming in pain is next level television"), and the crowd ("KEEP GOING! THIS IS AWESOME!").
- **The savers:** Abby (who walks the city all night looking for the boys), Huoyan (who refreshes his phone for news of Abby through a whole tournament), Lance (who saves ants, and Volkan), and first-chapter Kage (who wants his name to make rescued people laugh).
- **The test case:** Kage, who wants both. "He wanted this forever… the whole world would never stop cheering for him" (2.15). His best moment is a save almost nobody notices: a tongue stuck out at a scared kid (3.16).
- Lance says it outright: "Why the hell do you want to be number one anyway? He's not the guy who saves the most people."

## 2. Hands: holding on and letting go

The book's most consistent physical image.

- The Prologue: "His hand closed around a wrist… his fingers were holding air."
- 1.1: "All the alternate universes where his grip held."
- 1.2: "This time, he made sure his grip held."
- 1.3: "Kage could feel the emptiness in his hands again."
- 1.12: Thanatos's offered hand, "the hand that promised true growth."
- 1.13, 2.10, 2.15: Kage's hand "curled around nothing" each time Frostbite comes up.
- 2.12: "He closed his hand around the whistle" before driving his bone into Abaddon's throat.
- 3.5: Orion's "hand balled into a fist at his side before he made it let go."
- 3.16: Orion's fist closed around the whistle, raised high.
- 4.1: Ailani takes Kage's hand; he lets go to look at a cake; "You may not get the chance to hold it again." "I won't let go a second time! I swear!"
- 4.1 and 4.7: Ailani's power costs her her hands.

A boy whose power is attachment keeps failing to hold on. A girl whose power is creation pays for it with her hands. The book has built its theme into its characters' bodies.

## 3. Attachment, and the fear of it

Kage's power is adhesion. His life is detachment: "stopped unpacking his bag," nine girlfriends after Ailani, "We're not friends!" The book keeps asking whether he can stick to anyone. His closest bonds are the ones he refuses to name (Orion), the one he refused to accept until it was gone (Abby), and the one he keeps letting go of (Ailani).

## 4. Support, and what it costs

"Support" is one of the most loaded words in the book.

- Abby: "I'm training to be a support hero!" "I will be your number one supporter." She dies keeping that promise.
- Kage's power was classified as "rescue, support, and detaining," and he refuses the label.
- Lucifer: "You're a nothing support hero! The best doesn't need support!"
- C.O.R.E.: "I am supportive of her, not you."
- Zulfiya forfeits "to publicly show my support," and Kage screams at her: "YOU'RE NOT GOING TO LIVE YOUR LIFE TO SUPPORT MINE!"
- Elias promises to be "exclusively supportive."
- Rhatella supports everyone with gear and is supported by almost no one.
- Even the name Nasser can mean "supporter."

The book's argument: support given freely is the best thing a person can offer, and the most dangerous thing to accept, because people die giving it.

## 5. Atonement

Kage trains like "atonement with no finish line." Lance holds people to "the standard I didn't hold to myself" because of his father. Abby can't wait because she waited for her brother. Orion carries the dome. "I want to be number one because it's the only way to make up for the things I've done." "Same as you. I gotta make up for the wrongs I've done." Almost every major character is paying off a debt, and none of them can tell when it's paid.

## 6. Consent and bodies

The book returns again and again to bodies being used without permission.

- C.O.R.E. draws blood without asking.
- Anubis photographs students and asks for cameras in their dorms. Kage: "pictures of students without their consent."
- Kage hugs Ailani after she says to move.
- Zulfiya: "If he doesn't wish to be healed, he can't be forced."
- Thanatos rebuilds Kage's body. Frostbite: "You just invaded the boy's body without consent and forced yourself onto him."
- Abaddon occupies Lucifer's body; Lucifer can't sleep.
- Nerezza's game turns Kage's limbs into stakes.

## 7. The monster inside (deepened by Chapter 7)

Three boys carry a "someone else."

| The boy | The other | How it gets out | What it wants |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lucifer | Abaddon | Sleep, or acceptance | Freedom, recognition: "We are one!" |
| Kage | Thanatos's biomass | Injury, high heart rate, losing the whistle | To win, to dominate, to be seen ("He's worse.") |
| Lance | "King Arthur" | Fighting too long | To strike first; to fight, blind |

Each boy treats his other self differently. Lucifer imprisons his and gives in for a win. Kage hides his and lies about it. Lance stops himself before it takes over, and when it does, he apologizes. The book is asking what you owe the part of yourself that wants to win more than it wants to be good. Abaddon's title for this is "Giving Into Yourself."

## 8. "We're not friends"

The book's signature phrase, and its meaning keeps shifting.

- 1.7: Kage and Orion, in unison, for comedy.
- 2.1: "I'm happy to not be your friend, Lucifer." Non-friendship as a gift.
- 2.5: Lucifer to Abby: "I'm not your friend! I'm not even your acquaintance!" As cruelty.
- 1.15, 2.13: Orion to Huoyan and Ailani: "We were not and never were friends." As denial.
- 3.7: Kage: "Orion, who's not my friend, by the way."
- 3.16: Lucifer to Kage: "You see me smile and think we're friends? You're dreaming."
- 4.4: Kage to Orion: "Why would I do that? We're not friends." As a wound.
- 4.4: Ailani decodes it: "If that's how you act around your 'not friends', you admitting to being friends with her shows a lot of closeness."
- 4.6: Orion to Rebento: "We're not friends." As bitterness.
- 4.7: Ailani: "he still chose Orion. His 'not friend.'" As the truth.

In Kage's language, "friend" is rare and "not friend" is the deepest bond he has.

## 9. Royalty, titles, and names

- **Kings:** King Kage (Zulfiya), the "King" speech, Lance-who-knights (Cheyenne: "wouldn't that make him a king?"), Lance's "King Arthur," "A Kings' Quarrel," king-sized hotel beds.
- **Princes:** "enjoy being a prince" (Cheyenne), "Prince Kage for now," Zulfiya switching to "Prince."
- **Queens and emperors:** Reina (Spanish for "queen"), Thanatos Augustus ("Augustus" was the first Roman emperor's title).
- **Knights:** Knight Volkan, Sir Lance.
- **Bowing:** Kage bows to Huoyan (apology), to Frostbite ("reverential"), to Lance (apology). Zulfiya bows to Kage. Huoyan "genuflects" sarcastically to Gene. Lance promises to bow if Kage wins.
- **Hero names:** Sticky Situation (chosen to make people laugh), Hermes (a nickname turned real), Sugar Bomb, Hellfeather, Anubis ("Ours are kind of boring," Anubis admits), Shifting Horror, Frostbite.

## 10. Parents, present and absent

Kage has none, and the book makes the absence visible through other people's parents. Alex's mother: "I'm so proud of you for trying!" Gene correcting Geneki in front of millions. Anubis's brother refusing the family name. Lance's villain father, whose crimes were their only time together. Kelly's son, who doesn't show up. And Kage's substitutes: Thanatos ("like a father talking to his child"), Frostbite ("I'm not really good with kids," patting his head), Anubis (conditional love), Huoyan ("I sleep here"). Kage watching Alex's mother (3.7) and wondering what a proud father feels like (3.13) are two of the quietest, saddest beats in the book.

## 11. Honor versus results

Lance: "Win not because you bend the rules, but because you can show the world you're better than them." Anubis: "Win by any means necessary." Kage, in between: "It doesn't matter how I won. I still beat you." The tournament is a long argument between these positions, and nobody wins it cleanly. Lucifer breaks Reina's gear to win. Orion low-blows Inazuma. Lance spares Volkan. Kage breaks Elias's glasses, then keeps his promise to fight honestly, then perverts it into "Give me Abaddon."

## 12. Watching and surveillance

Anubis's drones and files. The "strange camera" Nerezza destroys. School cameras "top of the line and everywhere." Anubis "asking" for dorm cameras. The villains murdering politicians who push the "Security Accords" to put "cameras all over the hero city." Elias and Ailani watching Kage. Orion watching Kage, and hoping not to find anything. Kage watching his own mourners from the ceiling. The book's politics are forming around this: the heroes want to watch everyone, and the villains want the cameras gone.

## 13. Light and dark, angels and demons

Abby is an angel; her light hurts Nerezza, the darkness. Lucifer's name means light-bringer, his surname means firelight, and he carries the angel of the abyss. "Angels can't befriend demons." Kage, whose name means shadow, escapes Nerezza by flying toward the moon. Abby's "Divine Judgement" is light consuming the girl who made it.

## 14. Performance

The crowd, the cameras, the screens, the merch. Gene's announcer voice. Anubis's "showmanship." Kage's hero poses. Lance's "A show was asked of me, and a show I shall give!" Ailani's mirror room, her dream of choreography, and "This is my time, and I've been dying to perform." The crowd "treated [them] like wrestlers who finally got color on camera." Chapter titles like "Curtain Call" and "Dinner Needs A Show." The book is very aware that heroism, in this world, is a performance, and it's asking whether that's always a bad thing. Ailani's fight in 4.7 suggests performance can be honest: a dancer winning by counting beats.

## 15. Food and hunger

Kage's power runs on lipids: cheese, a minifridge, oil ("That shit is nasty"), "eat breakfast." Thanatos is "never finished consuming" and is left "starving" after the rebuild. Rebento's power runs on sugar. Ailani's honey toast, and "I gave you that to savor!" Elias's accusation: Kage "went to a hero school for food." Hunger is how the book talks about wanting.

## 16. Small creatures

Lucifer and Abaddon call Kage an ant, a termite, a bug, a worm, a cockroach, and "a bug that needs to be squashed before he cocoons and grows wings." Rebento: "we aren't just little beetles for him to step on." And Lance's origin story is saving ants from a frog. The book's answer is that the small things the strong want to step on are exactly who heroes are for. Ailani's crystal butterfly (2.3) and Abaddon's "cocoons and grows wings" (3.16) suggest a metamorphosis is coming.

## 17. Speed and slowness

Orion loves slow walks. Kage's power "is made to slow things down." Volkan is "the slow kid." Inazuma: "A speedster should never make things go slower." Orion's speed stretches the seconds before Ailani cries. Orion walks, "not ran," to deliver bad news. The fastest boy in the book is at his best when he chooses to slow down.

# 9. Motifs, Symbols, and Callbacks

## The gold whistle: a complete history

The whistle is the book's best object. Here's every appearance.

| Where | What happens |
|---|---|
| 1.5 | "Kage saw something gold sparkle down at the bottom" of Abby's backpack. |
| 2.2 | Abby gives it to him: "My brother made this whistle before he died… only I can hear it! If you ever need support or help from me, blow it!" Kage: "I'll be sure to use it a ton." |
| 2.5 | Abby thinks "maybe Kage would never blow it." |
| 2.6 | "His other hand brushed the whistle in his pocket. Abby could help, but no." He texts Thanatos instead. Abby comes anyway, and dies. |
| 2.6 | "Then, why did she give me this whistle?!" Orion: "She might've thought you'd never use it." |
| 2.10 | Kage blows it silently for days in a bathroom corner, then hangs it on a string around his neck. |
| 2.12 | It slips out onto the asphalt, "speckled with his blood." He closes his hand around it and fights on. |
| 2.14 | He clutches it when Orion asks about Abby. It's the only answer he gives. |
| 3.2 | He clings to it when Severine screams for him, then lets go and slides out of sight. |
| 3.2 | His fist clamps on it: "DO NOT TELL ME TO CALM DOWN!" |
| 3.3 | His hand drifts to it when Ailani calls him "cute," Abby's word. |
| 3.4 | He clutches it at the word "support." Ailani: "You told me that whistle was hers." |
| 3.9 | He clutches it and tries to hide it; Cheyenne notices; Orion intervenes. |
| 3.11 | During Elias's speech, his body screams to "grab the whistle." He doesn't. |
| 3.13 | He squeezes it while confessing he's "really bad at saving people." |
| 3.15 | He takes it off and throws it to Orion: "Keep that safe for me." Then reaches for it after Lucifer burns his face, finds nothing, laughs, and goes feral. |
| 3.16 | Orion balls a fist around it and raises it high: "the only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby." |
| 4.4 | Orion throws it back: "You look like you need it." |
| 4.6 | Kage's hand moves to it when Ailani and Lance corner him. |

The whistle is Abby's support, which Kage refused, then begged for, then wore, then lent to his rival, then lost control without. It's also the most important unresolved object in the book: a whistle only Abby can hear, blown for days, with no body found.

## The dolphin backpack

Abby carries water bottles in it (1.1, 1.5), and the whistle at the bottom. After Divine Judgement, it's all that's left: "One of the straps was torn off, and the backpack itself was singed and scorched." Kage wears it by its single strap, hugs it in the bathroom, refuses to throw it away ("Don't even think about touching it"). Where is it now? It isn't mentioned after 2.10. Did he bring it to the stadium? Is it in the warehouse? That's worth a line.

## Doors
