Ailani slams one (1.3), shuts another ("lose my number," 2.4), and nearly shuts a third on Orion (2.8). Abby and Orion each stop a door with their foot (2.5, 2.8). Kage breaks into Anubis's (1.14) and Lucifer's (2.11) and opens the stadium doors to a cheering crowd (2.15). Huoyan slams Anubis's on the way out (2.9). The stadium lobby door creaks open on Kage's return (2.13). Doors are where relationships open and close in this book.

## Walls

"You don't hang posters if you're not sure how long the walls are yours." Kage gets thrown into walls constantly ("Kage was getting tired of hitting walls," 4.6). Ailani's crystal walls. Nerezza's shadow wall twenty miles out. The scorched handprints on Anubis's desk. Kage clinging to the stadium wall instead of falling out. The book keeps asking which walls are his.

## Glass and windows

The convenience store windows "went white and burst outward." Severine's barrier shatters into fragments that "glitter" in the firelight. Nerezza goes through a window. The arena is under a dome of thick glass. Kage presses his hand to the glass when Severine screams, and when he sees Alex's mother. Ailani presses hers to it during the Lucifer fight. The glass separates the people who are watched from the people who watch.

## Mirrors and reflections

The Prologue ends with Kage looking at his reflection. Kage and Ailani reconcile in a mirror room. "That mirror definitely blushes every morning." Inazuma and Rebento are Kage's mirrors; Lance is his reversed mirror.

## Hair

Kage's lazy hair becomes his self-cut, floating, "action figure" hair, which looks like it's permanently mid-telekinesis. His hair stands up when he uses telekinesis. Zulfiya's hair heals. Huoyan slicks his back when angry. Orion fixes his dreads when uncomfortable. Ailani ties hers up before performing. Rebento: "If that even is his real name." Lucifer: "period hair."

## Eyes

Arctic blue (Kage), amber-gold (Orion), yellow (Abby), purple (Severine, Thanatos), light and dark blue, dark red (Volkan), one lazy eye (Geneki). Glowing yellow appears twice: Abby's eyes before Divine Judgement, and Lance's eyes in King Arthur. Kage hides his blue eyes behind yellow contacts on villain work. Abaddon's eyes "burn solid red." Kage's eyes go "feral" after the biomass flares.

## Teeth

Kage loses a tooth to Lucifer, spits it into Lucifer's eye, and learns from Zulfiya that it will never grow back. It's his first permanent cost, and Lance decides it doesn't make his smile less comforting.

## Food

Cheese (1.15), minifridges (3.12, 4.2), honey toast (4.1), a chocolate cake he lets go of Ailani's hand for (4.1), gumballs, jawbreakers, sprinkles, hard candy (Rebento), a strawberry lollipop (Cheyenne), ramen (1.6), overpriced concessions (3.8), a "shit brand" of beer (3.15), a perfect sphere of ice in a scotch (4.0).

## Head pats

Orion ruffles Rhatella's hair (1.4). Kage pats Abby's head (1.7). Thanatos pats Kage's (1.13) and Orion's (2.7). Lucifer pats Kage's to belittle him (2.4). Frostbite pats Kage's twice (2.10, 2.12). Kage pats unconscious Lucifer's (2.11). Anubis pats Kage's (2.15). Elias pats Reina's (3.2). Huoyan pats both boys' heads, then slams them into a wall (2.2). The head pat is a gesture of ownership and affection at once. Everyone pats this kid's head. Very few people ask him how he's feeling.

## Hats and crowns

Cheyenne's hat "she never took off." Kage steals it (3.9). The wager: if Kage loses, he wears it on the podium (4.4). A cowboy hat is the book's literal crown.

## Swords

The black claymore engraved "Minkowski," on holders "specifically forged" for it, one or two degrees crooked after Kage touches it. Kage uses it as a baseball bat. Lance's ten blades in 4.7. Lance knights Volkan with an arming sword.

## Baseballs

Elias throws a baseball out of a building without a bat (banked force). Kage swings the claymore like a baseball bat. Orion's baseball slide under the candy blast. Lance bats crystals back like baseballs. A small, oddly consistent motif.

## Insects and small creatures

Ant, termite, bug, worm, pebble, mosquito, cockroach, beetles, a bug that will cocoon and grow wings, a butterfly made of crystal, and the ants Lance saved from a frog.

## Every setup and payoff I found

| Setup | Payoff |
|---|---|
| "His fingers were holding air." (Prologue) | "This time, he made sure his grip held." (1.2) |
| "Smiling weapons with brand logos on their chests." (Prologue) | "Weapons who smiled because it was good for the branding." (1.12); Kage and Lucifer in hero merch (4.2) |
| "Windows went white and burst outward." (Prologue) | Frostbite's ice; Kage's hand curling around nothing (1.13, 2.10, 2.15) |
| "Sharp enough to ace anything." (Prologue) | The Hermes epithets (1.15); fire-stop caulk (3.15); the excretion loophole (3.11) |
| Abby: "Everything is going to be alright." (1.1) | Her last words (2.6); Kage's hero ideal (3.13) |
| "Another speedster, huh?" (1.2) | Inazuma (3.3) |

# 10. The Relationship Web

## The pairs

- **Kage and Orion.** Rivals who refuse to be friends. Fast vs. slow, brash vs. calm, improvise vs. execute. Each has hidden the worst thing he's done from the other.
- **Kage and Lucifer.** Both carry a monster. Both are pushed by Anubis's attention. Both call each other insect names (or have it called). They smiled at each other once, mid-fight, and stopped when they noticed.
- **Kage and Lance.** The villain's recruit and the villain's son. Lance escaped what Kage is walking into.
- **Kage and Inazuma, Kage and Rebento.** Two boys described as Kage with a different power: loud, provocative, self-damaging.
- **Anubis and Thanatos.** Two death gods, two mentors, two men who call Kage a "project."
- **Huoyan and Frostbite.** Fire and ice. Both gruff. Both care more than they show. Both pat a boy's head and then threaten him.
- **Abby and Kage.** Both haunted by someone they might have saved. Both refuse to wait (her) or to ask (him).
- **Abby and Lucifer.** Angel and demon. "I'm happy to not be your friend."
- **Elias and Lucifer.** Warden and prisoner, best friends. Elias stops things; Lucifer's demon is the thing that must be stopped.
- **Reina and Lucifer.** The only person who can take his heat.
- **Ailani and Orion.** Training partners and confidants. He loves her; she uses humor to grieve, as Kage taught her.
- **Ailani and Kage.** First love. The book's oldest wound and its most hopeful door.
- **Zulfiya and Kage.** The first to crown him, the first to state the rule of consent.
- **Cheyenne and Kage.** The one who lowered his crown.
- **Rhatella and Kage.** She understood his name; he broke her work. She wishes someone would fight over her.

## Who knows Kage's secret?

| Person | What they know | How |
|---|---|---|
| Orion | Kage is working with Shifting Horror; Kage "shouldn't be breathing" | Heard it in the dome (2.7); saw the injuries |
| Nerezza | Everything, plus Orion's choice | She was there |
| Thanatos, Frostbite | Everything | They made it |
| Elias | Kage is "hiding something" and "illogical" | Deduction (3.12) |
| Ailani | Something is "off"; "He's worse" | Orion's hints; observation |
| Rebento | The healing is "not natural" | Observation (3.16, 4.3) |
| Lucifer | Kage broke into his room and knocked him out | Abaddon showed him (3.16) |
| Anubis | Kage was on camera Thursday; Frostbite "attacked" him; "compromised" | Cameras, drone photo (1.14, 2.15) |
| Huoyan | Kage "got burned" before but not now | Observation (3.15) |
| Zulfiya | Possibly something | Her hair never touches him again |

## Who knows Orion's secret?

Only Nerezza and Thanatos. That's the most dangerous fact in the book.


# 11. Continuity Ledger

## What holds up (and is easy to miss)

I checked these specifically because they're easy to get wrong across 107,000 words. They're all consistent.

- **The calendar.** "Two months" plus "two more months" equals "four months." Huoyan's and Inazuma's "six months" agree. "Missing since Tuesday… three days" fits a Thursday scene. Nerezza's "a week" and Kage's "a little over a week" both fit. (See Part 3.)
- **Kage's fever ladder:** 102°, 105°, 110°. It only goes up, and it spikes again when he hardens concrete in 3.16.
- **Orion's speeds:** 200 normal, 300 at his broken limit, 100 with a passenger.
- **Heights:** Orion is seven inches taller than Kage and one inch taller than Lucifer, and Lucifer is annoyed by that inch. Elias is an inch taller than Reina.
- **Rhatella's alphabet.** Every Rhatella item belongs to a student before "O." Nobody after has her gear.
- **Kage's toenail glue.** Shoes off before every serious fight.
- **Huoyan's fireball vs. Lucifer's fire.** Kage burns in 2.2 and heals in 3.15, and Huoyan notices.
- **Elias's power rules.** Vital processes continue, so frozen people talk and Kage excretes. The clock burns only under resistance, so the long speech outlasts three minutes.
- **Lance's blades.** Cut rigid things, spare flesh, saw slowly: lethal to Volkan, saw-like against crystals.
- **Ailani's sigil.** Every use, including the time she holds back (1.3). It rotates for telekinesis in 2.3 and 4.7.
- **The out-of-bounds rule.** Touching under the arena floor counts as out, established by Geneki (3.3) and used by Ailani (4.7).
- **Anubis's drone.** It works when the Frostbite photo is taken (1.13–1.14), and it's destroyed the night Nerezza smashes "a strange camera" (2.6). That also explains why nobody saw Kage swing across the city carrying Lucifer.
- **Anubis knew Kage was alive.** He saw him on camera Thursday (2.15) and told no one, which explains his confidence and his smirk at Huoyan in 2.13.
- **The claymore.** Touched (2.11), noticed crooked (2.13), explained (3.16).
- **Lucifer's sleeplessness.** It explains Abby's diagnosis, his yawning, Elias sleeping over, and his strange energy after Abaddon was let out.
- **The tooth.** It never grows back, which matches Zulfiya's rule.
- **Ailani quoting Kage's exact promise** (1.3 in 2.4), and **Cheyenne quoting his exact King speech** (1.11 in 3.9).
- **Volkan's red eyes and hoodie** in 1.7 and 3.10.
- **"Hermes."** Zulfiya heard it in 2.2, which is why she understands in 2.14.
- **Kage's eyes.** Blue at school in 2.14, consistent with yellow contacts worn only "while out on patrol."
- **The Thursday night infiltration.** Ailani and Orion talk about "this weekend," the poster says "this weekend," and Anubis confirms "Thursday."

## What's been fixed since the last draft

- Kage's contacts changed from gray to yellow (and the blue-eyes question is covered by the patrol-only line).
- Lucifer says "Cruz" in the backroom deal.
- Cheyenne's quote of the King speech is now exact.
- Roughly half the dialogue-tag punctuation errors (about 54 down to 27).
- All curly quotes normalized.
- Several hyphenation and comma slips.

## What still wobbles

None of these are serious. They're the kind of thing a careful editor would flag.

1. **"No one else knew Kage called Orion that"** (L1599). Abby and Zulfiya both heard it in 2.2. The narration states as fact what's really Zulfiya's belief. Easy fix: "Zulfiya knew that name was private."
2. **How Lucifer knew Kage and Ailani were talking again** (2.4). They reconciled in a hidden room on Friday; by Monday he'd told her. Who told him?
3. **Gene's reason for switching the match order** (3.11). Huoyan asks "What makes this different?" and she doesn't answer. Decide whether that's characterization or a rescue.
4. **Abaddon's trigger.** "Fully asleep" in the card; voluntary in 3.16. Clarify.
5. **Abaddon's pronouns.** Third person outside, first person inside, mostly first person after "we are one." Probably deliberate. One line acknowledging it would lock it in.
6. **Avalon's Legacy's card** says it cuts "like it isn't even there," but the fights show it sawing. The cast-saw version is better.
7. **Severine's formality.** No contractions in her introduction ("I do not date fools"), but plenty later ("you're a riot," "I've talked to you"). Pick one.
8. **C.O.R.E.'s "no filed record of misconduct"** for Orion (1.4), the day after Huoyan warned him. Probably warnings aren't filed. Not worth fixing unless you want the joke.
9. **"Twenty selected" vs. "top class."** The Prologue says only twenty are selected; later chapters imply tiers ("top class," "lower class," "switch classes"). One line explaining the structure would settle it.
10. **Kage confronts Anubis about the files** (2.15), effectively admitting he broke in, and Anubis doesn't react.
11. **Huoyan hears Kage admit to the schoolyard fight** (4.2) and says nothing.
12. **Ailani knows the whistle was Abby's** (3.4), meaning Orion told her offstage.
13. **"I am Greek, Kage, not Roman"** (1.15) can read as an ethnicity claim. One clause ("My power's Greek…") clarifies.
14. **Elias's dorm.** Kage "had always assumed Elias and Lucifer shared a dorm" (2.11), but Elias seemed to be sleeping there in 2.4–2.5. It works if Elias sleeps over (he does), but a line saying so would help.
15. **Where is Abby's backpack?** Last seen in 2.10.
16. **Line-level slips:** "compliment" for "complement" (L3116); "Lucifer grit his teeth" for "gritted" (L3325); tense slips at L2801 ("keeps" for "kept") and L2807 ("wants," "needs" for "wanted," "needed"); a stray period in "Partner.," (L2743).

Everything the book has opened and hasn't closed. I'm listing them so none get lost.

**The world**
- Why does the UN allow only one hero program? (Prologue) Why does the UN approve Anubis's drone budget? (2.9)
- What are the Security Accords, and who's behind the murders? (4.0)
- What is the Elite Program, and who gets in? Gene promised to explain.
- How many classes are there, and what is the "top class"?

**Kage**
- Why did Kage refuse to explain his enhancement? (Prologue)
- Why is he a "human insulator"? Why does his hair stand on end, and why did he vibrate after collapsing? How did he survive 110°F? (1.4, 1.10, 2.2)
- What is in Rhatella's sample of his blood? (1.9)
- What does Thanatos know about "a boy who's gone through what he has"? (2.7)

- What does Thanatos need him to do? (2.7)
- What will he say when someone finally asks, in front of everyone, "Where's Abby?"

- The wager with Lance: a bow or a hat. (4.4)
- Rhatella's unfinished gear for him.

**Orion**
- His choice in the dome. Only Nerezza and Thanatos know.
- His crush on Ailani.

**Lucifer, Elias, Reina**

- Reina's anger at both boys. (4.3)
- Lucifer's future: "Huoyan's problem." (3.8)

# 13. Theories and Predictions

Ranked by how strongly the text points. I'm not trying to guess your plan. I'm telling you what a careful reader will see. If something here is wrong, the clues may be pointing somewhere you didn't intend.

## Strong (the text is clearly pointing here)

### 1. Frostbite was at the convenience store, and probably caused it.

- The Prologue: "the windows went white and burst outward." White, like frost on glass.
- Kage's hand "curled around nothing" three times, and every time it's about Frostbite: meeting him (1.13), seeing his bare hand (2.10), and hearing Anubis say his name (2.15). That's the hand that held the wrist.
- Kage "recognized" him but "couldn't quite put his finger on where," and decided it was the news.
- "There was something about Frostbite he really didn't like. He couldn't name it." (1.13)
- Frostbite's sudden kindness, and his apology: "I shouldn't be threatening your life." "That was the other half… At least, it was the half he was willing to say out loud."
- He bets on Kage and calls him "a student of mine."

If this is your plan, it's seeded beautifully. If it isn't, readers will assume it anyway.

### 2. Elias was there the last time Abaddon got out, and someone was hurt or killed.

- "Me and Elias have history." (2.1)
- "Not showing signs of trauma." (Anubis's file, 1.14)
- "Even fewer have lived after seeing it." (Hellspawn card)
- "Being out again after years." (Abaddon, 3.16)
- Elias runs to the bathroom shaking (3.16), then: "You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again." (4.3)
- He sleeps in Lucifer's room to keep watch.
- A claymore engraved with *his* family name hangs on custom holders in *Lucifer's* room, and Elias insists it hang perfectly straight.

My guess: the claymore belonged to a member of Elias's family who was present when Abaddon first emerged, and Lucifer keeps it as a memorial and a promise. Elias's power, which stops things in time, may be the only thing that can hold Abaddon, which is why Elias is always there.

### 3. Kage's power is more than adhesive.

- He "refused to explain" it (Prologue).
- He's a "human insulator" (1.4).
- His hair stands on end with telekinesis, like static (1.10, 1.15).
- He kept "shaking and vibrating" after collapsing (1.10).
- He survived 110°F: "maybe my DNA is meant for that" (2.2).
- Huoyan: "Simple telekinesis like that shouldn't cause even the most rookie student to collapse. Something's off." And: "I haven't told Anubis everything." (1.10, 1.11)
- Anubis: "stronger than originally thought." He was kept in the top class despite doing nothing.
- Huoyan's rule says body-based powers can't do telekinesis, and Kage's power is excretion, "super similar to sweating," which sounds body-based. Yet he has telekinesis. Maybe he's "two in one," like Huoyan.

I don't know the answer, but the clues suggest a second layer, possibly electrical.

### 4. Nerezza will reveal Orion's choice.

She knows it, she enjoys hurting both boys, she's coming back to the hero city for another assassination, and she has a "serious talk" planned with Thanatos. The worst possible timing would be right before or during a Kage–Orion match, which is exactly what Kage wants.

## Likely

### 5. Ailani reaches the grand final, or forces Orion to make an impossible choice.

Her section, her momentum, and her promise ("If we fought, I'd kick your ass, Kage!") all point to her facing Kage. But her hands are locked, and Orion loves her. The most interesting outcomes: Orion forfeits for her (a mirror of Zulfiya's forfeit, which Kage hated), or Orion wins and the ban becomes a public question.

### 6. Anubis suspects Kage's villain connection and is letting it happen.

He says "compromised" out loud and accepts Kage's cover. He saw Kage enter Lucifer's dorm and didn't report it. He has a photo from the Frostbite alley. "A hero who has secrets in his pocket is the best kind!" He may be using Kage, consciously or not, to find the people behind the Security Accords murders.

### 7. The boss has a personal interest in Kage.

Thanatos "needs" him. Nerezza "can't kill" him yet. Frostbite nearly says "I don't care what he—." Thanatos knows "what he's gone through." Kage is an orphan with no known history, which is exactly the kind of character whose parents turn out to matter.

### 8. Rhatella will find something in Kage's blood.

She has a sample from before the biomass. She's the only person who could analyze it. She missed the healing on TV by seconds, which keeps her ignorant until someone shows her. And she has a crush on him, which will make it hurt.

### 9. Lucifer joins Huoyan's agency.

"You'll officially be Huoyan's problem." Huoyan, a fire user, already wants him. Lucifer needs someone who understands living with fire.

### 10. Lance's father matters again.

Lance is a reformed thief's son who knows breaking and entering. Kage has broken into three rooms. And a "high earning villain" in Quebec may have connections to "the boss," who sends people to Russia and kills officials. A Lance–father scene, or Lance recognizing Kage's methods, feels inevitable.

## Possible

### 11. Abby isn't gone.

No body. She "burst completely into light." "Some are worried she may actually have fallen from heaven." "Not every ability of Abby is known." Kage blew a whistle only she can hear, for days. Her section kept going long after her death. My honest opinion: be careful. Her death is the best chapter in the book. If she returns, it has to cost something real. If she does come back, the whistle should be how.

### 12. Frostbite is connected to Lucifer, maybe as family.

He uses Lucifer's exact insult ("sticky fucking freak"). He crouched over the unconscious Lucifer "longer than required" and almost touched his face. He's ice; Lucifer is fire. His test was specifically "bring me Lucifer Tulivalo." "I'm not really good with kids." It's speculative, but the lingering is pointed. (Theories 1 and 12 can both be true.)

### 13. Anubis's power involves watching or judging.

The real Anubis weighed hearts to judge truth. This one catches lies instantly ("Kage, that's a lie"), has impossible camera angles, and his power "activated" as he said "My agency will be watching." He wants cameras everywhere. If his power is some form of remote sight or judgment, the drone would be a cover.

### 14. Cheyenne's power involves something on a string.

Her surname is an old word for a yo-yo. She jingles "like keys or pocket change." She put her hands in her pockets before attacking. She doesn't think her power can do telekinesis. My guess: something that returns, retracts, or snaps back.

### 15. Kelly's power involves borrowing.

His surname is the Irish word for "loan." He "had to use [his] enhancement" on Severine, who fights with repulsion. Maybe he borrowed it, or borrowed her strength. A power-borrower who refuses to teach and wants Anubis's spot would be interesting.

### 16. Alexander's power is plant-based.

Green hair, flower earrings, and a surname that means "grove." He never got to show it.

### 17. Youseff is stronger than he lets on.

Elias, who studied everyone, didn't study him. Anubis said his "heart's just not in it," not that he's weak. Huoyan smirked at his fake trip.

### 18. Kelly's son is the missing classmate.

Eighteen known students, twenty selected, and a son who "didn't end up showing up."

### 19. Huoyan and Anubis were a couple.

"Hon." "You've made that very clear. I am certainly not trying to rehash anything." "One day, I'm your sidekick." "You save that for when you're crying at home." "I sleep here." The subtext is strong and handled with restraint. I'd keep it that way.

## Predictions for the rest of the tournament

- **The half final (Orion vs. Ailani)** forces the love triangle into the open. Ailani's locked hands are the key complication. Either someone heals her (Zulfiya would be a meaningful choice) or she fights with crystal fists, which she's already shown she can do.
- **The grand final** is Kage against whoever wins. If it's Orion, the ban becomes a public question, and Anubis has to choose between rules and ratings. If it's Ailani, Kage has to choose between "I don't want to hurt you" and "Should a number one hero ever be number two?"
- **The wager.** I'd bet Kage loses the grand final and stands on the podium in Cheyenne's hat. A humbled Kage, wearing a friend's crown, would be the right ending for a section about someone who knew him before. But I'd understand going the other way and making his win the worse outcome. Lance bowing to a boy who's lying to him would be bitter.
- **After the tournament,** Orion tells Huoyan about Abby (as he promised Ailani), Thanatos returns from Russia furious with Frostbite, Nerezza has her "serious talk," and the Security Accords bring the villains back to the hero city.


# 14. Craft Notes

These are notes on the writing itself rather than the story. They're the most fixable things in the book, and fixing them would make the biggest difference to how professional it reads.

## Voice and tone

The book has two voices. The Prologue is literary: controlled sentences, concrete images, a cynical narrator. The chapters are bright, dialogue-heavy, and anime-flavored, with power cards, catchphrases, and all-caps shouting. Both are strong. The transition between them is abrupt.

The best chapters blend them. 2.6 (Abby's death), 2.7 (the rebuild), 2.8 (Orion in Ailani's room), and 3.16 (the mindscape) all use the chapters' energy with the Prologue's control. The rebuild in 2.7 is the best example: "the way a blacksmith feels the flaws in warped metal." And 4.7 shows a third mode, wordless and rhythmic, which is new and excellent.

The tonal range is a strength. The book moves from Rhatella shipping two boys to Abby dying to Kelly waking people with smelling salts, without losing the reader. That's hard to do.

## The power cards

The power-card introductions are a genre device, and you use it well.

- **Hero cards** are exclamatory and present tense, like a broadcast: "The girl is Abby Neosame! Her enhancement is Eyes of Divinity!"
- **Villain cards** are flat and factual: "This man is the villain Shifting Horror. His enhancement is Bio-Forge." No exclamation points at all. That contrast is one of the smartest formal choices in the book.
- **Cheyenne's card** is cut off mid-sentence when Elias freezes her: "Her name is Cheyenne Bandalore! Her enhancement is—." The narration itself gets frozen.
- **Ailani's card** is written in its own time period: "The freshman class's Ailani Shoma!"
- **Cards are held back** for characters whose powers are mysteries (Anubis, Youseff, Cheyenne, Kelly, Alexander, Gene).

Two suggestions. First, trim each card to its rules plus one flourish; several end with two or three sentences of praise. Second, keep the cards' promises exact: Avalon's Legacy says it cuts rigid things "like it isn't even there," but the fights show it sawing slowly. The fights are better than the card, so update the card.

## Exclamation points and capitals

The manuscript has **2,142 exclamation points** in about 107,000 words, roughly one every 50 words, and **671 fully capitalized words** of four letters or more.

The power cards earn their exclamation points. So do Gene's announcements, Abaddon, and real screaming. But a lot of ordinary dialogue and narration also ends in "!", and when everything is loud, the loud moments lose their force.

**Suggestion:** in dialogue, keep exclamation points for shouting and genuine surprise. In narration, remove almost all of them. Then reread the Abaddon fight, and notice how much louder his capitals feel.

## Dialogue tag punctuation

You've fixed about half of these since the last draft. About **27** remain, and 24 of them are in Section 3, Chapter 11 or later. Examples:

- "…this choice is the most logical." Orion said, trying to sound confident. (L1220)
- "The claymore. It's crooked." he said, pointing. (L1547)
- "I'm supposed to be fighting Reina right now." Lucifer said while trying to relax. (L2811)
- "No. He doesn't." Elias said flatly. (L3181)
- "I trust Kage." Lance said to silence the murmuring room. (L3632)

The rule: if the words after the quote are a speech verb (said, replied, answered, asked, yelled, chimed in), end the quote with a comma and keep the tag lowercase if it's a pronoun: `"No. He doesn't," Elias said flatly.` If the words after are an action ("Elias adjusted his glasses."), a period is right. A search for `." ` followed by a name or pronoun and a speech verb will find almost all of them. The full list is at L1220, 1227, 1547, 2811, 2813, 2846, 2899, 2930, 3181, 3203, 3369, 3375, 3478, 3497, 3530, 3550, 3558, 3572, 3578, 3580, 3632, 3646, 3650, 3692, 3714, 3748, and 3754.

## Other line-level fixes

- **L3116:** "didn't compliment her intimidating form" should be "complement."
- **L3325:** "Lucifer grit his teeth" should be "gritted."
- **L2801:** "frustrated he keeps pushing her away" should be "kept."
- **L2807:** "While, of course he wants to win… he needs to go all in on tranquility" should be "wanted" and "needed."
- **L2743:** "That's better than a prince, Partner.," has a stray period.
- **"Woah" vs. "Whoa":** 17 to 3. Pick one.
- **Ellipses:** both "…" and "..." appear. Pick one.
- **The "..?" construction** (a hesitant question, like "Hermes..?") appears about 30 times. It's a real signature of the book's voice, and I like it, but at 30 it's become a habit. Keep it for the moments that need it.
- About ten double spaces.
- Dialogue colloquialisms like "Have I spoke," "must've ate my dust," and "what you say to who" are fine. They're character voice.

## Repeated beats

| Word | Count |
|---|---|
| nodded | 84 |
| laughed | 65 |
| chuckled / chuckling | 45 |
| sighed | 31 |
| grin | 28 |
| smirk | 24 |
| giggled | 21 |
| shrugged | 14 |
| facepalmed | 12 |
| sucked his teeth | 10 |

None of these are wrong, but some scenes stack three or four in a row. When everyone nods, nobody's nod means anything. Try cutting about half of the nods and chuckles and replacing some with nothing at all. Most dialogue doesn't need a gesture.

## Telling after showing

The book often shows a feeling and then names it in the next sentence. In 2.14, the hug breaks the tension, and then the narration says, "The tension in the room drained from the hug, and everyone relaxed." In 2.6, Abby's last words already echo the first time she met Kage, and then the narration says, "They were the same words she'd said the day they met." When your showing is good (and it usually is), trust it. Cutting the naming sentence will usually make the moment land harder.

## Point of view

The book uses a roaming third-person narrator that moves between heads, sometimes within a paragraph. That's a legitimate choice, and it's how the book can show the hero lounge reacting to a fight in the same scene. Sometimes, though, the head-hopping blurs whose scene it is (3.4's Orion–Ailani conversation is a good example). One technique: give each scene a "home" character, and only leave that head for a clear reason.

There's also an interesting pattern in who gets interiority. Kage and Orion get the most. Ailani gets surprisingly little, even in her own section. Abby gets one paragraph (2.5), and it's devastating. If Section 4 is Ailani's, give her the interior access the boys have had.

## Speeches

The characters give long speeches: Kage's King speech, Thanatos's offer, Elias's humiliation of Kage, Anubis's succession pitch, Abaddon in the mindscape, Lance's backstory, Frostbite's "strategy vs. strength." Most are excellent. Several run a third longer than they need to. The best one, Lance's ants and the frog, works because it's short and specific. Try cutting each long speech by a third and keeping every line that's concrete.

## Dialogue voices

The loud characters (Kage, Rhatella, Rebento, Inazuma, Cheyenne, Gene, Abaddon) have distinct voices. The articulate characters (Orion, Lance, Elias, sometimes Huoyan and Anubis) can sound alike: full sentences, careful logic, formal diction. Give each a verbal habit.

- **Elias** already has one: literal and precise ("Affirmative," "If I said it's crooked, then it's crooked").
- **Lance** could lean more old-fashioned, as Kage teases ("Indubitable!").
- **Orion** could be shorter and drier ("Sir, respectfully, I told you something like this would happen" is his best register).
- **Huoyan** is a non-native English speaker, which Anubis corrects. Use that occasionally in his lines.
- **Severine** should keep her no-contractions rule.
- **Anubis** says "Sigh" out loud. Keep that.

## Pacing

Sections 1 and 2 are well paced. Section 3's first round is deliberately anticlimactic, and you've strengthened the scenes between matches in this draft. If you want to tighten further, the hero-lounge banter in 3.1 and 3.3 could lose about a quarter. Section 4 moves well, and its newest chapter is its fastest.

## Length and structure

At about 107,000 words with the tournament unfinished, this is already a full-length novel, and the story clearly continues past the tournament (Thanatos's return, the Security Accords, the boss). For publication, you'll likely want to split it. The cleanest break is probably the end of the tournament, wherever that lands in Section 4. That would make Book One "the year Kage started trying," ending on the podium, with the villains and secrets lined up for Book Two.

# 15. The Chapter Title Decoder

Your chapter titles are one of the book's quiet pleasures: puns, ironies, callbacks, and a few that only make sense after you've read the chapter. Here's what each one is doing.

| # | Title | What it's doing |
|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | Nice To Meet You | Literal (Kage meets Orion and Abby), and a little ironic: they're about to beat each other up. |
| 1.2 | Birth Of A Rivalry | Straightforward. The rivalry is born affectionate ("I look forward to it"). |
| 1.3 | A Beautiful Past | Pure irony. The past is the breakup and nine girlfriends. |
| 1.4 | Rhat Tech | "Rat" + "high tech." A one-word joke and a perfect intro to Rhatella. |
| 1.5 | Stronger Than Strong | Kage's vow, quoted. |
| 1.6 | The Race | Plain, and right. The best set piece in Section 1 doesn't need a pun. |
| 1.7 | Class In Session | Literal, plus a sense that Kage is about to learn something (about Lucifer). |
| 1.8 | Perfectionist Meets Wildcard | Severine meets Kage. "Wildcard" returns as Rebento's accusation in 4.3. |
| 1.9 | Rhatella In A Novella | A rhyming pun on her romance novel. |
| 1.10 | Will Without A Way | Kage's will is enormous; his way (telekinesis) fails. |
| 1.11 | Bow To Your King | Zulfiya's bow, and the first "King." |
| 1.12 | Whatever It Takes | Kage's ethic, and the price of the handshake. |
| 1.13 | Partner, Not Pawn | Kage's demand. The villains agree to his face and laugh behind his back. |
| 1.14 | Anubis-pheric Pressure | "Atmospheric pressure" with Anubis inside it. The best pun title in the book. |
| 1.15 | You're My Problem | Kage's "My issue is you!", and Huoyan making Kage Orion's problem. |
| 2.1 | Patient Confidentiality | Abby's medical ethic, and the secret she keeps until she dies. |
| 2.2 | No More Fighting | The ban. Also a hope the book immediately betrays. |
| 2.3 | Arts And Crafts | Glue and a crystal butterfly: literal craft supplies. |
| 2.4 | That Didn't Last Long | The reconciliation, over by Monday. |
| 2.5 | Go Help Someone Else | Lucifer's words to Abby in 2.1, and the reason she goes to Kage. |
| 2.6 | Divine Intervention | Abby, the angel, intervenes, with "Divine Judgement." |
| 2.7 | Fractured Brought Whole Again | Kage's broken body, rebuilt by Thanatos. "Whole" is generous. |
| 2.8 | About Kage… | The conversation Orion has to have. The ellipsis is the hesitation. |
| 2.9 | You're Supposed To Know | Huoyan's accusation: "You're supposed to know where everyone is all the time, Mr. Know-it-all!" |
| 2.10 | Another Task | Frostbite's test, and an echo of the first "mission." |
| 2.11 | Appreciate The Invite, Luci | Kage's line as he carries Lucifer off with the stolen poster. |
| 2.12 | What's Hiding Underneath | Abaddon inside Lucifer, and the biomass inside Kage. |
| 2.13 | Sophomores Are Tournament Bound | Literal, and the start of the big set piece. |
| 2.14 | He's Back | Everyone's reaction, compressed. |
| 2.15 | The Talk And Walk | A twist on "walk and talk": Anubis's interrogation on the camaraderie walk. |
| 3.1 | And…Begin! | Gene's starting call. |
| 3.2 | Quick And Painful | Lucifer's and Geneki's matches, and Severine's meltdown. |
| 3.3 | Which Kage Is Best? | Inazuma's question about maid Kage vs. desk-nap Kage, which Kage hates. |
| 3.4 | Forfeit Versus Failure | Zulfiya's forfeit and Cheyenne's failure, side by side. |
| 3.5 | Just Like You Both | Inazuma is "like you if you had Orion's enhancement." |
| 3.6 | Faster Than Fast | Orion vs. Inazuma, echoing "Stronger Than Strong." |
| 3.7 | Gone In An Instant | Two matches end in seconds. |
| 3.8 | Dinner Needs A Show | The crowd is eating overpriced food and wants entertainment. The deal is the show. |
| 3.9 | A Lighter Crown | Cheyenne trades Kage's "King" for "Prince." |
| 3.10 | Honoring A Snail's Pace | Lance honors Volkan ("Sir Salyangozu"), and slows down to do it. |
| 3.11 | Studying Is Important | Elias "studied" everyone except Kage, and lost to the one he didn't study. |
| 3.12 | The Whole Truth | What Reina isn't being told, and what Elias deduces Kage is hiding. |
| 3.13 | A Kings' Quarrel | Two "kings," Kage and the knight-making Lance, argue about honor. The apostrophe is correct for two kings. |
| 3.14 | Honor Is For Suckers | Lucifer breaks Reina's gear the morning after Lance's honor lecture. And a sucker is also a lollipop, like Cheyenne's. |
| 3.15 | Paid In Exposure | Three meanings: fame as payment, Kage's biomass exposed, and Abaddon about to be exposed to millions. |
| 3.16 | Giving Into Yourself | Lucifer accepting Abaddon. Also Kage, giving in to what the biomass wants. |
| 4.0 | Chapter NULL: The World's Been Watching | A chapter outside the count, where the audience lives. |
| 4.1 | Honey Toast | The first date, and the smell of bread and honey from 1.3. |
| 4.2 | The People Want More | Gene asks the crowd whether to continue. They want more. |
| 4.3 | Can You Outrun Explosions? | Rebento's threat to Orion, and the explosions in the lobby. |
| 4.4 | Sweeter Than Sugar | Cheyenne to Lance, right before Sugar Bomb's fight. |
| 4.5 | Running Off Those Calories | A sugar pun for a speedster vs. a candy bomber. |
| 4.6 | Curtain Call | Ailani's exit line: "I've been dying to perform." |
| 4.7 | Saw Right Through You | Lance's blades saw; Ailani sees through his tells; Lance goes blind; she saws through his plan. |

A few that could be sharper: "Which Kage Is Best?" promises a bigger debate than the chapter delivers. "Sophomores Are Tournament Bound" is the plainest title in Section 2. But overall, the titles are a real strength, and the best ones ("Anubis-pheric Pressure," "Paid In Exposure," "Saw Right Through You") work on several levels.

# 16. Closing Verdict

## The ten best moments

1. **Abby's death** (2.6). "It's okay, Kage…everything is going to be alright."
2. **The count to three** (2.7). Orion chooses Kage to suffer, logically, and carries it alone.
3. **The tongue** (3.16). Kage sticks his tongue out at a scared kid while a demon slams him into the floor, and the kid laughs, and then cheers.
4. **Orion raises the whistle** (3.16). "Orion's fist was the only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby."
5. **"I remembered to eat breakfast!"** (3.11). A payoff 2,500 lines in the making.
6. **The terrible-name callback** (3.6). "DUDE, THAT HERO NAME IS TERRIBLE! PICK ANYTHING ELSE!"
7. **"This time, he made sure his grip held."** (1.2). The Prologue's wound, answered in the first fight.
8. **The ceiling** (2.11). Kage, stuck to the ceiling, watches Orion punch a wall and swear at him for dying.
9. **The ants and the frog** (3.13). Lance's origin, in one small, perfect image.
10. **"Two counts to her one, and Ailani had been counting beats her whole life."** (4.7). The newest chapter's best line, and the moment Section 4 becomes hers.

Honorable mentions: the scorched handprints (2.9); "I sleep here" (2.13); the knighting of Volkan (3.10); the vote, with Elias and Lucifer's hands up (4.3); Kage blowing a silent whistle for days in a bathroom corner (2.10); the frozen power card (3.4); the scotch ice that won't melt (4.0).

## The best lines

- "You don't hang posters if you're not sure how long the walls are yours."
- "When the dust cleared, his fingers were holding air."
- "A name that can turn something horrifying into something funny."
- "Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life."
- "People will call me King Kage one day. That's not arrogance, by the way. It's a spoiler."
- "The way ambition propped up exhaustion like splints on a failing structure. A boy trying to outrun collapse by sprinting toward it."
- "Animals that never stop running don't evolve. They die."
- "I'm happy to not be your friend, Lucifer."
- "At the end of the day, angels can't befriend demons."
- "Monsters don't fear other monsters. They recognize each other."
- "You can't break…what's already broken!"
- "You weren't Sticky Situation in your first year."
- "Oh, the straight man doesn't get to be funny too? You're putting me in slots, Kage."
- "Okay, Anubis."
- "I try to hold people to the standard I didn't hold to myself."
- "Children screaming in pain is next level television."
- "You let go of my hand to ogle at a cake? You know, you may not get the chance to hold it again."
- "Orion's fist was the only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby."
- "Two counts to her one, and Ailani had been counting beats her whole life."

## What I want next

1. **Ailani's interior life.** We've watched her fight. Now let us inside her head when she faces Orion.
2. **Orion alone with his secret.** He hasn't had an interior chapter since 2.8.
3. **Somebody says "Where's Abby?" out loud,** in front of everyone. Only Huoyan is still looking.
4. **Frostbite's "other half."** The book promised it.
5. **Rhatella.** She has Kage's blood, a broken heart, and two destroyed inventions. Bring her back.
6. **Kage almost confessing to Lance,** and stopping. Lance would recognize his breaking-and-entering methods.
7. **The racism thread, resolved.** An apology from Inazuma, or Kage owning that he walked away.
8. **A cost Kage can't regenerate.** He's lost a tooth. Next, it should be something in a relationship.
9. **Lance and King Arthur.** What it is, whether it's his power or his psychology, and whether Cheyenne knows.
10. **The grand final,** wherever it lands. And the wager: a bow or a hat.

## Final word

This book has a real heart, a strong spine, and an unusually careful memory. The setups and payoffs alone show a writer who thinks about the whole book while writing each page. You revised in the right places this draft: the first round of the tournament reads better, the callbacks click louder, and the new chapter is the cleanest and most elegant thing in the manuscript. What the book needs now is polish at the sentence level, and that's the easiest kind of work to do once the story is this solid.

It's a story about a boy who wanted people to laugh when he saved them, and who is being slowly turned into something that wants them to watch him win. Around him, a speedster is hiding the worst choice of his life, a demon boy has just shown the world what lives inside him, a knight has discovered a king inside himself, and a dancer is about to step into the ring with her hands turned to crystal. The book knows exactly what it's doing. I want to read what happens next.

**Overall: 8.5/10.**
